(squares without depicted 2-morphisms commute, i.e. have invertible 2-morphisms)

Figure 1:
Illustration of the different categories appearing in Proposition
2.58
.

Proposition 2.58
.

Let
F,G\in\Cat^{I}_{\infty}
. There are canonical isomorphisms:

\displaystyle\Hom^{\lax}_{\Cat^{I}_{\infty}}(F,G)

\displaystyle\cong\Dinat^{\oplax,2-\pseudo}(\pi_{1}^{*}F,\pi_{2}^{*}G)\cong\oplaxlim^{2-\pseudo}_{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I}\Hom(F(i),G(i^{\prime}))

\displaystyle\Hom^{\oplax}_{\Cat^{I}_{\infty}}(F,G)

\displaystyle\cong\Dinat^{\oplax,1-\pseudo}(\pi_{1}^{*}F,\pi_{2}^{*}G)\cong\oplaxlim^{1-\pseudo}_{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I}\Hom(F(i),G(i^{\prime}))

The same holds true with lax and oplax interchanged.
The terms on the right might be called
(op)lax ends
of
\Hom(F,G)
as functor on
I^{\op}\times I\to\Cat_{\infty}
.

Mutatis mutandis the Proposition holds also for
\infty
-operads:

Proposition 2.59
.

Let
I
be an
\infty
-operad and
F,G\in(\Cat_{\infty},\times)^{I}
.

\displaystyle\Hom^{\lax,\inert-\pseudo}_{(\Cat_{\infty}^{\times})^{I}}(F,G)

\displaystyle\cong\oplaxlim^{\inert-\pseudo,2-\pseudo}_{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I}\Hom_{\Cat_{\infty}^{\times}}^{p(\mu)}(F(i),G(i^{\prime}))

\displaystyle\Hom^{\oplax,\inert-\pseudo}_{(\Cat_{\infty}^{\times})^{I}}(F,G)

\displaystyle\cong\oplaxlim^{\inert-\pseudo,1-\pseudo}_{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I}\Hom^{p(\mu)}_{\Cat_{\infty}^{\times}}(F(i),G(i^{\prime}))

Notice that the expression
\Hom_{\Cat_{\infty}^{\times}}^{p(\mu)}(F(i),G(i^{\prime}))
factors through

{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I\to\Cat_{\infty}

as shown in Lemma
2.53
.

Proof.

Follows immediately from Proposition
2.58
, the discussion in
2.55
and basic facts on operads
2.19
.
∎

Remark 2.60
.

In the special case
F=\cdot
we get, in particular:

\displaystyle\oplaxlim_{I}(G)\cong\oplaxlim^{1-\pseudo}_{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I}(\pi_{2}^{*}G),

\displaystyle\oplaxlim_{I}(G^{\op})\cong(\laxlim_{I}(G))^{\op}\cong(\oplaxlim^{2-\pseudo}_{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I}(\pi_{2}^{*}G))^{\op}.

Translated to the corresponding (co)fibrations this gives a way to relate cofibrations of operads with fibrations of cooperads, which will be at the heart of the (co)bar constructions, see
3.4
.

2.61.

We need a simple fact about compositions of fibrations that is, however, a bit tricky to state:
Recall that the composition
K\to J\to I
of two fibrations is a fibration. If
J\to I
is classified by
G:I^{\op}\to\Cat_{\infty}
and
K\to J
is classified by

F:J^{\op}=(\oplaxcolim_{I^{\op}}G)^{\op}\to\Cat_{\infty}
then
K\to I
is classified by a functor
I^{\op}\to\Cat_{\infty}
which is a left oplax Kan extension along
J^{\op}\to I^{\op}
of
F
. It maps
i\mapsto\oplaxcolim_{G(i)^{\op}}F|_{G(i)^{\op}}
. We get a corresponding functor between categories of sections

\Hom_{\Cat_{\infty/I}}(I,K)\to\Hom_{\Cat_{\infty/I}}(I,J).

(22)

This has fibers over
\alpha:I\to J
given by
\Hom_{\Cat_{\infty/I}}(I,\alpha^{*}K)=\oplaxlim_{I}F\circ\alpha^{\op}

where
\alpha^{*}K
is defined by the Cartesian diagram

\textstyle{\alpha^{*}K\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{I\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{J}

actually because the whole
\Hom
-categories commute with fiber products (and not just their groupoids of invertible morphisms).
We claim: The construction gives a functor

\displaystyle\Hom_{\Cat_{\infty/I}}(I,J)^{\op}

\displaystyle\to\Cat_{\infty},

\displaystyle\alpha

\displaystyle\mapsto\oplaxlim_{I}F\circ\alpha^{\op},

and the functor (
22
) is a fibration classified by this functor. A rigorous proof is omitted for the moment.
Translated purely in terms of the classifying functors it means:

Proposition 2.62
.

Let
G\in\Cat^{I^{\op}}_{\infty}
be a functor and
F\in\Cat^{(\oplaxcolim_{I^{\op}}G)^{\op}}_{\infty}
a functor. Then there is a canonical isomorphism:

\boxed{\oplaxlim_{i\in I^{\op}}(\oplaxcolim_{x\in G(i)^{\op}}F(i,x))\cong\oplaxcolim_{(x(i)\in G(i))_{i}\in(\oplaxlim_{I^{\op}}G)^{\op}}(\oplaxlim_{i\in I^{\op}}F(i,x(i))).}

A similar statement holds true with a set
C
of morphisms in
I
:

\oplaxlim_{i\in I^{\op}}^{C-\pseudo}(\oplaxcolim_{x\in G(i)^{\op}}F(i,x))\cong\oplaxcolim_{(x(i)\in G(i))_{i}\in(\oplaxlim^{C-\pseudo}_{I^{\op}}G)^{\op}}(\oplaxlim_{i\in I^{\op}}^{C-\pseudo}F(i,x(i))).

Remark 2.63
.

One can also construct the map (almost) purely by abstract adjunction properties of lax limits and colimits. Because we have for
F:I\to\Cat_{\infty}
:

\oplaxcolim F=\mathop{\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\int$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\int$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\int$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\int$}}}}F

and

\oplaxlim F=\Hom_{\Cat_{\infty}/I^{\op}}(I^{\op},\mathop{\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\int$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\int$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\int$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\int$}}}}F)

we have a canonical morphism

\eval:I^{\op}\times\oplaxlim F\to\oplaxcolim F.

Then consider the diagram

\textstyle{I\times\oplaxlim_{I^{\op}}G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eval}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{1}}
\textstyle{\oplaxcolim_{I^{\op}}G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{\oplaxlim_{I^{\op}}G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{I\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{\cdot}

The non-horizontal functors are either cofibrations (the map
\pi
) or projections (in particular, fibrations and cofibrations).
Therefore there exist fiber-wise oplax limits and colimits (oplax Kan extensions) along these maps which we denote here by
p_{!}
and
p_{*}
.
They form 2-adjunctions

\Cat_{\infty}^{I,\oplax}\leftrightarrow\Cat_{\infty}^{J,\oplax}

in the sense that there is an isomorphism of
\infty
-categories (not just groupoids)

\Hom_{\mathcal{C}^{I}}^{\oplax}(F,\alpha^{*}G)\cong\Hom_{\mathcal{C}^{J}}^{\oplax}(\alpha_{!}F,G).

Therefore, we can calculate with them like with usual Kan extensions. First obviously

\pi_{1}^{*}p^{*}\cong\pi_{2}^{*}p^{*}

therefore there is a mate

p^{*}p_{*}\Rightarrow\pi_{2,*}\pi_{1}^{*}

which is an isomorphism because
\pi_{2,*}
is computed fiber-wise.
Hence we get a morphism

p_{!}\pi_{2,*}\Rightarrow p_{*}\pi_{1,!}

and finally:

p_{!}\pi_{2,*}\eval^{*}\Rightarrow p_{*}\pi_{1,!}\eval^{*}\Rightarrow p_{*}\pi_{!}

This is the functor in the statement.

Proposition 2.64
.

Let
F\in\Cat^{I}_{\infty}
be a functor and

X,Y\in\oplaxlim_{I}F

then we have a canonical equivalence

\Hom_{\oplaxlim_{I}F}(X,Y)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\lim_{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I}\Hom_{F(i)}(X(i),F(\mu)Y(i^{\prime}))=\lim_{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I}\Hom_{\oplaxcolim_{I}F}^{\mu}(X(i),Y(i^{\prime}))

where
\mu:i\to i^{\prime}
denotes an object in
{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I
.

Proof.

This has nothing to do with fibrations and is a simple consequence of the end formula for morphisms in functor categories.
Actually, for any functor
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{S}
, for
X,Y\in\mathcal{C}^{I}_{S}
, for
S\in\mathcal{S}
, we have

\Hom_{\mathcal{C}^{I}_{S}}(X,Y)=\lim_{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I}\Hom_{\mathcal{C},S(\mu)}(X(i),Y(i^{\prime})).

Indeed, we have

\Hom_{\mathcal{C}^{I}}(X,Y)\cong\lim_{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I}\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}(X(i),Y(i^{\prime}))

and, by definition, the Cartesian diagram

\textstyle{\Hom_{\mathcal{C},S(\mu)}(X(i),Y(i^{\prime}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}(X(i),Y(i^{\prime}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\{S(\mu)\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Hom_{\mathcal{S}}(S(i),S(i^{\prime}))}

Applying
\lim_{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I}
, we get a Cartesian diagram

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 63.28346pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-63.28346pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\lim_{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I}\Hom^{S(\mu)}_{\mathcal{C}}(X(i),Y(i^{\prime}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 87.28346pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.68945pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 87.28346pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Hom_{\mathcal{C}^{I}}(X,Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 121.15443pt\raise-24.68945pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-15.22154pt\raise-32.68945pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\{\id_{S}\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 89.76959pt\raise-32.68945pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 89.76959pt\raise-32.68945pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Hom_{\mathcal{S}^{I}}(S,S)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\\[0.0pt]
\end{gathered}\qed

Corollary 2.65
.

Let
I
be a small
\infty
-category.
For a functor
G\in\Cat^{I}_{\infty}
there is an isomorphism

\oplaxlim_{I}{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}G\cong{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}(\oplaxlim_{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I}\pi_{2}^{*}G)^{{(1,2),(2,1)-\pseudo}}

where the RHS is the full subcategory of objects
X\to Y\in{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}(\oplaxlim_{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I}\pi_{2}^{*}G)
such that
X\in(\oplaxlim_{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I}^{2-\pseudo}\pi_{2}^{*}G)
and
Y\in(\oplaxlim_{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I}^{1-\pseudo}\pi_{2}^{*}G)
.
Similarly, let
I
be a small
\infty
-operad. For a functor
G\in(\Cat_{\infty},\times)^{I}
, inducing
G^{\times}:I\to\Cat_{\infty}^{\times}\to\Cat_{\infty}
classifying the categories of operators, there is an isomorphism

\oplaxlim_{I}^{\inert-\pseudo}{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}G^{\times}\cong{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}(\oplaxlim_{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I}^{\inert-\pseudo}\pi_{2}^{*}G^{\times})^{{(1,2),(2,1)-\pseudo}}

where
{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I
means twisted arrow category in the sense of
\infty
-operads (
2.47
), observing that in all other occurrences it is applied to just
\infty
-categories.

Proof (sketch):

Consider the functor
\mlq\Hom\mrq:(\oplaxcolim_{I}G\times G^{\op})^{\op}\to\Gpd_{\infty}
given by
\Hom
fiber-wise, where
G^{\op}
is the
composition
of
G
with
\op
. To see that this is a valid morphism, observe that

\mlq\Hom\mrq\in\Hom^{\lax}_{\Cat^{I}_{\infty}}(G^{\op}\times G,\pi_{I}^{*}\Gpd_{\infty})\cong\Hom_{\Cat_{\infty}}(\laxcolim_{I}G^{\op}\times G,\Gpd_{\infty}).

\cong\Hom_{\Cat_{\infty}}((\oplaxcolim_{I}G\times G^{\op})^{\op},\Gpd_{\infty})

Apply Proposition
2.62
with
(I,G,F)
being
(I^{\op},G\times G^{\op},\mlq\Hom\mrq)
:

\oplaxlim_{I}(\underbrace{\oplaxcolim_{G(i)^{\op}\times G(i)}\mlq\Hom\mrq}_{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}G(i)})\cong\oplaxcolim_{(X,Y)\in(\oplaxlim_{I}G\times G^{\op})^{\op}}(\oplaxlim_{I}\Hom(X(i),Y(i))).

Using Remark
2.60
identify

\oplaxlim_{I}G\times\oplaxlim_{I}G^{\op}\cong\oplaxlim_{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I}^{1-\pseudo}\pi_{2}^{*}G\times(\oplaxlim_{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I}^{2-\pseudo}\pi_{2}^{*}G)^{\op}.

(23)

We have to show that there is a commutative diagram

\textstyle{(\oplaxlim_{I}G\times\oplaxlim_{I}G^{\op})^{\op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\oplaxlim_{I}\Hom}
\textstyle{(\oplaxlim_{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I}^{1-\pseudo}\pi_{2}^{*}G)^{\op}\times\oplaxlim_{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I}^{2-\pseudo}\pi_{2}^{*}G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Hom}
\textstyle{\Hom(I,\Gpd_{\infty})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lim}
\textstyle{\Gpd_{\infty}}

By Proposition
2.64
, and using the identification
{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}({}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I)={}^{\uparrow\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow}I
(denoting an object by
\textstyle{i_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{1}}
\textstyle{i_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{2}}
\textstyle{i_{3}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{3}}
\textstyle{i_{4}}
), and denoting by
\widetilde{X},\widetilde{Y}
the image of the pair
X,Y
under (
23
):

\Hom_{\oplaxlim_{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I}}(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{Y})\cong\lim_{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow}I}\Hom_{G(i_{4})}(\widetilde{X}(i_{1}\to i_{4}),G(\mu_{3})\widetilde{Y}(i_{2}\to i_{3}))

\cong\lim_{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow}I}\Hom_{G(i_{4})}(X(i_{4}),G(\mu_{3})G(\mu_{2})Y(i_{2}))\cong\lim_{{}^{\downarrow\downarrow}I}\Hom_{G(i_{4})}(X(i_{4}),G(\mu)Y(i_{2}))

(using Lemma
2.50
and numbering
{}^{\downarrow\downarrow}I
with the corresponding subindices of
{}^{\uparrow\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow}I
) and this is

\cong\lim_{I}\Hom_{G(i)}(X(i),Y(i)).

In the operad-case we get by Proposition
2.62

\oplaxlim_{I}^{\inert-\pseudo}(\underbrace{\oplaxcolim_{G(i)^{\op}\times G(i)}\mlq\Hom\mrq}_{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}G(i)})\cong\oplaxcolim_{(X,Y)\in(\oplaxlim_{I}^{\inert-\pseudo}G\times G^{\op})^{\op}}\oplaxlim_{I}^{\inert-\pseudo}\Hom(X(i),Y(i)).

and refining the argument above:

\oplaxlim_{I}^{\inert-\pseudo}{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}G\cong{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}(\oplaxlim_{(\mathrm{tw}^{\op}I)^{\op}}^{\inert-\pseudo}\pi_{2}^{*}G)^{{(1,2),(2,1)-\pseudo}}.

Then notice that

\displaystyle\oplaxlim_{(\mathrm{tw}^{\op}I)^{\op}}^{\inert-\pseudo}\pi_{2}^{*}G=\Hom_{\mathrm{tw}^{\op}I}(\mathrm{tw}^{\op}I,\oplaxcolim\pi_{2}^{*}G)^{\inert-\cocart}

\displaystyle=\Hom_{I}(\mathrm{tw}^{\op}I,\oplaxcolim_{I}G)^{\inert-\cocart}

and that the morphism that go to isomorphisms under
\mathrm{tw}^{\op}I\to{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I
are precisely the type-1 inert morphisms which go thus to isomorphisms, hence by Lemma
2.53
this is the same as

\displaystyle\cong\Hom_{I}({}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I,\oplaxcolim_{I}G)^{\inert-\cocart}

\displaystyle\cong\Hom_{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I}({}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I,\oplaxcolim\pi_{2}^{*}G)^{\inert-\cocart}\cong\oplaxlim_{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I}^{\inert-\pseudo}\pi_{2}^{*}G.\qed

2.8
Relative (operadic) Kan extensions

Let
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{S}
and
\alpha:J\to I
be functors of
\infty
-(co)operads, the latter small.
Let
S\in\mathcal{S}^{I}
. By pre-composition, it gives rise to a functor

\alpha^{*}:\mathcal{C}^{I}_{S}\to\mathcal{C}^{J}_{\alpha^{*}S}.

Definition 2.66
.

If a left adjoint
\alpha_{!}^{(S)}
(resp. right adjoint
\alpha_{*}^{(S)}
) of
\alpha^{*}
exists, we call it a
relative left (resp. right) Kan extension
.

Note that this comprises, in particular, the functors called
operadic Kan extensions
by Lurie
[
41
, 3.1.2]
.
Various sufficient criteria for their existence will be discussed in Sections
2.9
–
2.11
.

2.67.

We call a relative left Kan extension, if it exists,
fiber-wise
, if for any object
i\in I_{[1]}
, the mate

\colim_{J_{i}}\iota_{i}^{*}\to i^{*}\,\alpha_{!}^{(S)}

where
\iota_{i}:J_{i}\hookrightarrow J
is the inclusion, is an isomorphism. Similarly for right Kan extensions.
Somewhat informally, we say that a relative left or right Kan extension is
point-wise
, if there is a simple formula à la Kan, cf. Proposition
2.76

for
i^{*}\,\alpha_{!}^{(S)}
.

2.9
Relative Kan extensions — fiber-wise case

Proposition 2.68
.

Let
\mathcal{C},I,J,K
be (
\infty
-)(co)operads, the latter three small. Let
\mathcal{C}\to I
be a functor and

\textstyle{J\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{I}

be a morphism of (
\infty
-)exponential fibrations over
I
.

1.

If
\mathcal{C}\to I
is
L
-admissible (Definition
2.31
) and

\alpha

is (
\infty
-)coCartesian (Definition
2.41
). Then
the functor
\alpha^{*}:D_{I}(K,\mathcal{C})\to D_{I}(J,\mathcal{C})
has a left adjoint in the
(\infty,2)
-category
\mathrm{(co)Op}_{\infty/I}
(resp. in the
(1,2)
-category
\mathrm{(co)Op}_{/I}
).

2.

If
\mathcal{C}\to I
is
R
-admissible (Definition
2.31
) and

\alpha
is (
\infty
-)Cartesian (Definition
2.41
). Then
the functor,
\alpha^{*}:D_{I}(K,\mathcal{C})\to D_{I}(J,\mathcal{C})
has a right adjoint in the
(\infty,2)
-category
\mathrm{(co)Op}_{\infty/I}
(resp. in the
(1,2)
-category
\mathrm{(co)Op}_{/I}
).

Proof.

The functor
\alpha
can be seen as a morphism in
\Hom^{\oplax,\inert-\pseudo}_{\Cat^{\PF,\times}}(\Xi_{K},\Xi_{J})
or (via passing to the mate) as
\Hom^{\oplax,\inert-\pseudo}_{\Cat^{\PF,\times}}(\Xi_{J},\Xi_{K})
.
The functor in question is given equivalently by applying
L
to the first or
R
to the second (cf. Proposition
2.42
, assuming that
\mathcal{C}\to I
is
L
-, resp.
R
-admissible).
If
\alpha
is (
\infty
-)coCartesian we can also apply
R_{\mathcal{C}}
to the first, being a natural transformation (not only oplax) in this case. This yields a right adjoint. If
\alpha
is (
\infty
-)Cartesian, we can apply
L_{\mathcal{C}}
to the second, being a natural transformation (not only lax) in this case. This yields a left adjoint.
∎

Corollary 2.69
.

1.

If
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{S}
is
L
-admissible (Definition
2.31
) and
\alpha:J\to K
is a (
\infty
-)Cartesian morphism of (
\infty
-)exponential fibrations over
I
. Then
for each
S\in\mathcal{S}^{I}
, the functor

\alpha^{*}:\mathcal{C}^{K}_{p_{K}^{*}S}\to\mathcal{C}^{J}_{p_{J}^{*}S}

has a left adjoint
\alpha_{!}^{(p_{J}^{*}S)}
, i.e. a relative left Kan extension exists. It is computed fiber-wise over
I
, i.e. the mate

\colim_{J_{i}}\iota_{i}^{*}\to i^{*}\alpha_{!}^{(S)}

where
\iota_{i}:J_{i}\to J
denotes the inclusion of the fiber, is an isomorphism.

2.

If
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{S}
is
R
-admissible (Definition
2.31
) and
\alpha:J\to K
is a (
\infty
-)coCartesian morphism of (
\infty
-)exponential fibrations over
I
. Then
for each
S\in\mathcal{S}^{I}
, the functor

\alpha^{*}:\mathcal{C}^{K}_{p_{K}^{*}S}\to\mathcal{C}^{J}_{p_{J}^{*}S}

has a right adjoint
\alpha_{*}^{(p_{J}^{*}S)}
, i.e. a relative (operadic) right Kan extension exists. It is computed fiber-wise over
I
, i.e. the mate

i^{*}\alpha_{*}^{(S)}\to\lim_{J_{i}}\iota_{i}^{*}

where
\iota_{i}:J_{i}\to J
denotes the inclusion of the fiber, is an isomorphism.

Proof.

We can pullback
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{S}
along
S:I\to\mathcal{S}
which preserves
L
/
R
-admissibility, and assume w.l.o.g. that
\mathcal{S}=I
and
S=\id
.
Then we have
\mathcal{C}^{K}_{p_{K}}=\Hom_{\Cat_{/I}}(I,D_{I}(K,\mathcal{C}))
and
\mathcal{C}^{J}_{p_{J}}=\Hom_{\Cat_{/I}}(I,D_{I}(J,\mathcal{C}))
by the universal property of the Day convolution. Hence
the adjunction from Proposition
2.68
gives the required Kan extension adjunction.
∎

2.70.

Let
p:J\to I
be an (
\infty
-)exponential fibration.
Then the functor is, in particular, a morphism of (
\infty
-)exponential fibrations.
It is (
\infty
-)Cartesian (Definition
2.41
), if

\alpha^{\bullet}\,p_{J_{i}}\cong p_{I_{i}}

and (
\infty
-)coCartesian (Definition
2.41
), if

{}^{t}\!p_{J_{i}}\,\alpha^{\bullet}\cong\,{}^{t}\!p_{I_{i}}.

Obviously a fibration is always
\infty
-Cartesian and a cofibration is
\infty
-coCartesian.
A fibration is (
\infty
-)coCartesian if and only if the pull-back functors are (
\infty
-)cofinal and
a cofibration is (
\infty
-)Cartesian, if and only if the push-forward functors are (
\infty
-)final.
This shows the following:

Remark 2.71
.

Let
\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S},I,J
be (
\infty
-)(co)operads (all of the same type), the latter two small.

Let
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{S}
and
J\to I
be (co)fibrations of (
\infty
-)(co)operads (not necessarily of the same fibration type), we may summarize the assumptions of Corollary
2.69
as follows:

1.

Relative left Kan extensions (that are computed fiber-wise) exist along
J\to I
, if
\mathcal{C}
has cocomplete fibers, and

fibration

cofibration

fibration

pull-backs for
J\to I

always

are (
\infty
-)cofinal

cofibration

both properties hold

push-forwards for
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{S}

are (
\infty
-)cocontinuous

2.

Relative right Kan extensions (that are computed fiber-wise) exist along
J\to I
, if
\mathcal{C}
has complete fibers, and

fibration

cofibration

fibration

pull-backs for
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{S}

both properties hold

are (
\infty
-)continuous

cofibration

always

push-forwards for
J\to I

are (
\infty
-)final

Note that it does not matter whether the objects are operads, or cooperads, respectively, apart from the fact that fibrations of operads as well as cofibrations of cooperads are not
as commonly considered as the other cases.

2.10
Relative Kan extensions — point-wise case

The calculus of left relative Kan extensions is particularly rich for
L
-admissible cofibrations of
\infty
-operads
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{S}
. Recall that
L
-admissible here means
that the fibers are cocomplete and that the push-forward functors commute with colimits (argument-wise).
Similarly, of course, the same holds for right relative Kan extensions for
R
-admissible fibrations of
\infty
-cooperads.
In particular, they always exist, and a point-wise formula holds true (analogous to Kan’s formula for usual Kan extensions). See Proposition
2.76
below.

Definition 2.72
.

Let
I
and
J
be
\infty
-operads. A
generalized morphism

I\to J
is a 2-commutative diagram

\textstyle{I\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\Downarrow}
\textstyle{J\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\mathcal{O}

such that
\alpha
maps inert morphisms to inert morphisms and such that the 2-morphism consists of active morphisms. Denote the corresponding
(\infty,2)
-category by

\Op_{\infty}^{g}
.

Definition 2.73
.

Let
\mathcal{S}
be an
\infty
-operad.
We define the
(\infty,2)
-category
\Dia(\mathcal{S})
as the full subcategory of the lax slice category
\Op_{\infty}^{g}//\mathcal{S}
in which
the objects are honest morphisms of operads with small source. Similarly, we define
\Dia^{\op}(\mathcal{S})
as the oplax slice category.

A morphism in
\Dia(\mathcal{S})
is thus represented by a diagram

\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\Downarrow^{\mu}}
\textstyle{N\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{S}}

in which
\mu
consists point-wise of active morphisms. If
\mu
is an equivalence, we say that the morphism is
of diagram type
. The corresponding
\alpha
is then necessarily
an honest morphism of operads.

2.74.

Let
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{S}
be a cofibration of
\infty
-operads. It gives rise to a functor of
(\infty,2)
-categories

y(\mathcal{C}):\Dia(\mathcal{S})\to\Cat_{\infty}

mapping
(I,S)
to
\mathcal{C}^{M}_{S}
, the fiber of
\mathcal{C}^{I}\to\mathcal{S}^{I}
over
S
.
A morphism of diagram type
\alpha:I\to J
is mapped to the functor ‘composition with
\alpha
’:

\alpha^{*}:\mathcal{C}^{J}\to\mathcal{C}^{I}.

A morphism with components
\alpha=\id_{I},\mu:X\to Y
is mapped to the corresponding push-forward functor

\mu_{\bullet}:\mathcal{C}^{I}_{X}\to\mathcal{C}^{I}_{Y}.

Definition 2.75

((Co)operadic comma category)
.

For two morphisms of
\infty
-(co)operads
J\to I
and
K\to I
we define a
(co)operadic comma category
together with a 2-commutative diagram
in the 2-category
\Op_{\infty}^{g}
:

\textstyle{I\times_{/J}K\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\Swarrow}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{1}}
\textstyle{I\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{K\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{J}

where the morphism
\pi_{1}:I\times_{/J}K\to I
is only a generalized morphism of operads in the sense of Definition
2.72
.

For any
S\in\mathcal{S}^{J}
this yields a diagram in
\Dia(\mathcal{S})
of the form

\textstyle{(I\times_{/J}K,\pi_{2}^{*}\beta^{*}S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Swarrow^{\mu}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\pi_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{2}}
\textstyle{(I,\alpha^{*}S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(K,\beta^{*}S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{(J,S)}

We can define it as the limit of the following diagram of (co)operads and generalized morphisms:

\textstyle{I\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{}^{\downarrow\downarrow}J\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{2}}
\textstyle{J}
\textstyle{K\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{J}

where
{}^{\downarrow\downarrow}J
has been defined in Definition
2.47
.
One can also mimic the definition of
{}^{\downarrow\downarrow}J
and define a functor of operads

\displaystyle K

\displaystyle\to(\Cat_{\infty},\times)

\displaystyle k

\displaystyle\mapsto I_{\act}\times_{/J_{\act}}\beta(k)\qquad\text{usual comma category}

using factorization into inert and active.
I\times_{/J}K\to K
is then the associated cofibration (unstraightening).

Proposition 2.76

(relative (operadic) Kan formula)
.

If
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{S}
is an
L
-admissible cofibration of
\infty
-operads (or just
\infty
-categories) then relative left Kan extensions exist along any functor
\alpha:I\to J
of small
\infty
-operads whatsoever.
Moreover, there is an isomorphism

j^{*}\alpha^{(S)}_{!}\cong\colim_{I\times_{/J}j}S(\mu)_{\bullet}\pi_{1}^{*}

(24)

for objects
k\in K
and
S\in\mathcal{S}^{K}
.

There is a similar dual statement for
R
-admissible fibrations of
\infty
-cooperads.

Proof.

We have an adjunction in the
(\infty,2)
-category
\Dia(\mathcal{S})

\textstyle{(I\times_{/J}J,\pi_{2}^{*}S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\pi_{1}}=(\pi_{1},S(\mu))}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces(I,\alpha^{*}S)}
\scriptstyle{(\iota,\id)}

and an isomorphism

\alpha_{!}^{(S)}\cong\pi_{2,!}^{(S)}\,\iota_{!}^{(\pi_{2}^{*}S)}

in the strong sense that the existence of the RHS adjoints implies the existence of the LHS.
By the adjunction in
\Dia(\mathcal{S})
, we have

\iota_{!}^{(\pr_{2}^{*}S)}\cong\widetilde{\pi_{1}}^{*}=S(\mu)_{\bullet}\,\pi_{1}^{*}

giving the formula:

\alpha_{!}^{(S)}\cong\pi_{2,!}^{(S)}\,S(\mu)_{\bullet}\,\pi_{1}^{*}

\pi_{2}
is a cofibration of cooperads and thus a relative Kan extension exists and is computed fiber-wise by Proposition
2.69
.
The formula (
24
) follows.
∎

Example 2.77

(Free algebra)
.

Consider the morphism
\cdot\to\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}
where
\cdot
is the final category. Let
(\mathcal{C},\otimes)
be a monoidal

\infty
-category such that
\otimes
commutes with colimits (in each variable separately). Then the left relative Kan extension

\mathcal{C}\to(\mathcal{C},\otimes)^{\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}}=\Alg(\mathcal{C},\otimes)

exists. Notice that this is (by definition) a free algebra functor (left adjoint to the forgetful functor). Examining the proof, is suffices to see that
\mathcal{C}
has countable coproducts and that

\otimes
commutes with them (in each variable separately). Formula (
24
) is the usual formula for the free algebra.
For, observe that

\cdot\ \times_{/\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}}\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}\cong(\mathbb{N}_{0},+)

(as discrete operad). If
\otimes
does not commute with countable coproducts then the construction of a left Kan extension becomes more difficult but not always impossible (see Section
2.11
and Section
3.7
for a dual example).

2.11
Relative Kan extensions — general case

The last section settled the case of left Kan extensions along arbitrary functors
I\to J
of
\infty
-operads w.r.t. an
L
-admissible cofibration
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{S}
of
\infty
-operads (say).
The
L
-admissibility of course can be weakened to the existence of
certain
colimits (depending on
I\to J
) and the commutation of the push-forwards in
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{S}
with those.
Often times, however, the push-forward in
\mathcal{C}

does not commute with these colimits
!

For example, dually, the functor “cofree coalgebra” is a special case of a right relative Kan extension. Its construction by the general machinery accordingly assumes that
\otimes
commutes with countable products. This already fails in
(\Ab,\otimes)^{\vee}
.

However, assuming (w.l.o.g. — by using the yoga of Section
2.10
) that
I\to J
is a cofibration, and using the equivalence of
3.4
, we may pass to the corresponding
fibration

\mathcal{C}^{\vee}\to\mathcal{S}^{\op}
:

\textstyle{\mathcal{C}^{I}_{\alpha^{*}S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\alpha^{*}}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}J\times_{J}I,2-\cart}_{\pi_{2}^{*}\alpha^{*}S^{\op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\alpha^{\prime})^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}^{J}_{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}J,2-\cart}_{\pi_{2}^{*}S^{\op}}}

where
{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}J\times_{J}I\to{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}J
is a cofibration (sic) of cooperads constructed similarly to the operadic comma category (cf. Definition
2.75
).

Assume, that the fibers of
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{S}
have all relevant colimits (but no commutation!).
Then, for fibrations, there is no further obstacle to construct left Kan extensions, hence a left adjoint
(\alpha^{\prime})_{!}^{(\pi_{2}^{*}S^{\op})}
exists (Corollary
2.69
and especially Remark
2.71
).
However, it is only a functor

(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}J\times_{J}I,2-\cart}_{\pi_{2}^{*}\alpha^{*}S^{\op}}\to(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}J}_{\pi_{2}^{*}S^{\op}}.

It will land in the full subcategory
(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}L,2-\cart}_{\pi_{2}^{*}S^{\op}}
of 2-Cartesian objects precisely if the commutativity of the push-forward in
\mathcal{C}
with (the relevant)
colimits is satisfied. Hence this seems like circular reasoning. However, we gained some additional information:

Proposition 2.78
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{S}
be a cofibration of
\infty
-operads with cocomplete
19
19

19

or such that at least the relevant colimits exits…
fibers (not necessariy
L
-admissible).
Then relative left Kan extensions along the cofibration
I\to J
exists, if the
fully faithful inclusion

(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}J,2-\cart}_{\pi_{2}^{*}S^{\op}}\hookrightarrow(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}J}_{\pi_{2}^{*}S^{\op}}

has a (partial) left adjoint (a Cartesian projector) defined on the essential image of
(\alpha^{\prime})_{!}^{(\pi_{2}^{*}S^{\op})}
.

As an illustration we will construct the cofree coalgebra using the (dual) classical cobar construction in section
3.7
.

2.12
Pairings

Definition 2.79

(cf.
[
41
, Definition 5.2.1.5]
)
.

A fibration of
\infty
-categories

\mathcal{P}\to\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{D}^{\op}

with groupoid fibers, or equivalently, a functor

\mathcal{C}^{\op}\times\mathcal{D}\to\Gpd_{\infty}

is called a
pairing
of
\infty
-categories. An object
M\in\mathcal{P}
over
(C,D)
is called
left universal
, resp.
right universal
, if it
is a final object in the fiber over
C
, resp.
D
. The pairing is called
left (resp. right) representable
if for each
C
there exists a left universal object over
C
(resp. for each
D
there exists a right universal object over
D
).

The distinguished example is the twisted arrow category
{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}^{\op}
. Here
\id_{C}
is left universal over
C\in\mathcal{C}
and right universal over
C\in\mathcal{C}^{\op}
. A pairing which is left (resp. right) representable gives rise to a functor

F:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D},

respectively

G:\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{C},

in such a way that the pairing is classified by the functor

C,D\mapsto\Hom_{\mathcal{D}}(FC,D)\quad\text{resp.}\quad C,D\mapsto\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}(C,GD).

In particular, if
\mathcal{P}
is left and right representable then
F
is left adjoint to
G
. The distinguished example corresponds to
F
and
G
being both the identity

\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}
. This characterizes the distinguished example up to equivalence:

Lemma 2.80

(
[
41
, Corollary 5.2.1.22]
)
.

T.f.a.e. for a left and right representable pairing:

1.

The functors
F
and
G
are equivalences;

2.

The pairing is isomorphic to the distinguished one given by
{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}^{\op}
;

3.

An object of
\mathcal{P}
is left universal if and only if it is right universal.

If these conditions are satisfied the pairing is called
perfect
.

3
Classical and Lurie’s (co)bar

This chapter, which forms the abstract heart of these lectures, discusses the definitions of the derived and classical bar and cobar constructions.
It contains an existence proof of classical cobar in full generality, and an existence proof of derived bar and cobar for monoidal
\infty
-categories.

3.1
The general classical and derived (co)bar

In this section, we define the notion of
classical
bar and cobar constructions with cobar left adjoint (mainly for 1-(co)operads, but not necessarily) and the

derived
bar and cobar constructions with cobar right adjoint. The latter are a generalization of Lurie’s definition.
We consider an arbitrary cofibration of
\infty
-operads

\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{S}
, classified by a functor of
\infty
-operads
\Xi:\mathcal{S}\to(\Cat_{\infty},\times)
.
Denote by
\mathcal{C}^{\downarrow\uparrow}
the cofibration given by composing
\Xi
with the functor

I\mapsto{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I
(twisted arrow category, cf. Section
2.6
).

Denote by
\mathcal{C}^{\vee}\to\mathcal{S}^{\op}
the
fibration of cooperads
which is classified by the same functor
\Xi
. Then
(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{\op}\to\mathcal{S}
is the cofibration classified by composing
\Xi
with the functor

I\mapsto I^{\op}
.

We thus get a diagram of cofibrations of operads over
\mathcal{S}

\textstyle{\mathcal{C}^{\downarrow\uparrow}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{2}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{\op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{S}}

(25)

Notice that
\mathcal{C}^{\downarrow\uparrow}
is
not
the twisted arrow category
{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}\mathcal{C}
of the operad
\mathcal{C}
in the sense of Section
2.6
unless
\mathcal{S}
is the terminal
\infty
-category.

3.1.

For each small
\infty
-operad
I
and
S\in\mathcal{S}^{I}
this gives rise to a diagram

\textstyle{(\mathcal{C}^{\downarrow\uparrow})^{I}_{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{2}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}^{I}_{S}}
\textstyle{((\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{I^{\op}}_{S^{\op}})^{\op}}

(26)

The derived bar and cobar adjunction can be derived from the fact that that this
is a pairing of
\infty
-categories which is left and right representable. We will, however, proceed to give a more explicit definition of the
bar and cobar functors and then prove that (
26
) is isomorphic to the left- and right representable pairing defined by their adjunction.

In the special case
\mathcal{S}=I=\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}
and
S=\id
, the diagram (
26
) is the one of Lurie
[
41
, Theorem 5.2.2.17]
:

\textstyle{\Alg({}^{\downarrow\uparrow}\mathcal{C})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Alg(\mathcal{C})}
\textstyle{\Coalg(\mathcal{C})^{\op}}

because the cofibrations
\mathcal{C}\to\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}
,
(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{\op}\to\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}
, and
\mathcal{C}^{\downarrow\uparrow}\to\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}
, are just monoidal
\infty
-categories in this case and their associated
\infty
-categories (forgetting the monoidal structure) are just
\mathcal{C}_{[1]},(\mathcal{C}_{[1]})^{\op}
and
{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}(\mathcal{C}_{[1]})
, respectively. The monoidal structure are, in all cases, the obvious induced ones, e.g. for
{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}(\mathcal{C}_{[1]})
:
(X_{1}\to Y_{1})\otimes(X_{2}\to Y_{2})=(X_{1}\otimes X_{2}\to Y_{1}\otimes Y_{2})
.

3.2.

For
S\in\mathcal{S}^{I}
, there are two natural diagrams

\textstyle{(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I}_{\pi_{2}^{*}S^{\op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\pi_{1}^{*}}}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{2}^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}^{I}_{S}}
\textstyle{((\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{I^{\op}}_{S^{\op}})^{\op}}

\textstyle{\mathcal{C}^{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I}_{\Pi_{2}^{*}S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Pi_{2}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\Pi_{1}^{*}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}^{I}_{S}}
\textstyle{((\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{I^{\op}}_{S^{\op}})^{\op}}

(27)

where
\Pi_{2}^{*},\pi_{2}^{*}
are of the form discussed in (
2.74
) (precomposition) but
\widetilde{\Pi_{1}^{*}}
and
\widetilde{\pi_{1}^{*}}
are slightly twisted variants (see Definition
3.5
below).
See Section
2.6
for the definition of the twisted arrow (co)operads
{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I
and
{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I
for operads
I
.
The diagrams (
27
) are linked to the preceding (
26
) by the following:

Proposition 3.3
.

The pairing (
26
) is isomorphic to the pairing defined by the two (isomorphic) groupoid valued functors

\displaystyle(\mathcal{C}^{I}_{S})^{\op}\times(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{I^{\op}}_{S^{\op}}

\displaystyle\to\Gpd_{\infty}

\displaystyle(C,D)

\displaystyle\mapsto\Hom_{(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I}_{\pi_{2}^{*}S^{\op}}}(\widetilde{\pi_{1}^{*}}C,\pi_{2}^{*}D)\cong\Hom_{\mathcal{C}^{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I}_{\pi_{2}^{*}S}}(\Pi_{2}^{*}C,\widetilde{\Pi_{1}^{*}}D).

Before giving the proof, we have to discuss the precise definition of the functors
\widetilde{\pi_{1}^{*}}
and
\widetilde{\Pi_{1}^{*}}
.

3.4.

To define the functors
\widetilde{\pi_{1}^{*}}
and
\widetilde{\Pi_{1}^{*}}
and to prove Proposition
3.3
and Lemma
3.16
, first observe the following.
Let
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{S}
be a cofibration of
\infty
-operads which is classified by a functor of
\infty
-operads
\Xi:\mathcal{S}\to(\Cat_{\infty},\times)
. We can associate with it a fibration of cooperads
\mathcal{C}^{\vee}\to\mathcal{S}^{\op}
which is classified by the same functor. It may be described more directly by the following procedure:
Let
I
be a small operad and consider the pull-back:

\textstyle{\mathcal{C}^{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I,2-\cocart}_{\pi_{2}^{*}\mathcal{S}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}^{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I,2-\cocart}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{S}^{I}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{2}^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{S}^{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I}}

Here
2-\cocart
denotes the full subcategory of those functors mapping type-2 morphisms (Definition
2.49
) to coCartesian ones. Then we have for the fibers

(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{I^{\op}}_{S^{\op}}\cong\mathcal{C}^{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I,2-\cocart}_{\pi_{2}^{*}S}

the identification being given by the composition:

\textstyle{(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})_{S^{\op}}^{I^{\op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\pi_{2}^{*}S}^{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I,2-\cocart}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\Hom^{\oplax,\inert-\pseudo}_{(\Cat^{\times}_{\infty})^{I}}(\cdot,\Xi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\Hom^{\lax,\inert-\pseudo,2-\pseudo}_{(\Cat_{\infty}^{\times})^{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I}}(\cdot,\pi_{2}^{*}\Xi)}

where the bottom equivalence is Proposition
2.59
.

Definition 3.5
.

Using the discussion in
3.4
, the functor
\barconst:=\widetilde{\pi^{*}_{1}}

\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{S}^{I}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})_{\pi_{2}^{*}S^{\op}}^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I,2-\cart}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})_{\pi_{2}^{*}S^{\op}}^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I}}

is called the
classical bar construction
, and a left adjoint (if it exists) will be called the
classical cobar construction
.
Dually, the functor
\barconst^{\vee}:=\widetilde{\Pi^{*}_{1}}
is the composition

\textstyle{(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})_{S^{\op}}^{I^{\op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\pi_{2}^{*}S}^{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I,2-\cocart}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\pi_{2}^{*}S}^{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I}}

is called the
dual classical bar construction
, and a right adjoint (if it exists) will be called the
dual classical cobar construction
.

The names are justified by the discussion in Chapter
6
where we will see that these functors are closely related (up to composition with total decalage, and its right adjoint, respectively) to the classical bar and cobar constructions of
Kan for simplicial groups/sets and Eilenberg-MacLane and Adams for dg-(co)algebras.

It would be equally reasonable to discuss the functor
\widetilde{\Pi_{2}^{*}}
(dual bar construction) and its (potential)
right
adjoint, but this was rarely done classically. As an illustation, however, section
3.7
uses this dual cobar construction to construct the cofree coalgebra.
Any statement concerning them can abstractly be obtained by
replacing
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{S}
with
(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{\op}\to\mathcal{S}
. This interchanges Lurie’s bar and cobar constructions but
not
the classicial bar and cobar constructions!

Corollary 3.6
.

The following are equivalent:

1.

The pairing (
26
) is left representable;

2.

\widetilde{\Pi_{1}^{*}}
has a (partial) left adjoint defined on the essential image of
\Pi_{2}^{*}
;

3.

\pi_{2}^{*}
has a (partial) left adjoint defined on the essential image of
\widetilde{\pi_{1}^{*}}
.

and dually:

1.

The pairing (
26
) is right representable;

2.

\widetilde{\pi_{1}^{*}}
has a (partial) right adjoint defined on the essential image of
\pi_{2}^{*}
;

3.

\Pi_{2}^{*}
has a (partial) right adjoint defined on the essential image of
\widetilde{\Pi_{1}^{*}}
.

Definition 3.7
.

We call the composition

\barlurie:=\pi_{2,!}^{(S^{\op})}\ \widetilde{\pi_{1}^{*}}

the
derived (Lurie) bar construction
if
\pi_{2,!}^{(S^{\op})}
(relative left Kan extension, cf.
2.66
) exists on the image of
\widetilde{\pi_{1}^{*}}
.
We call the composition

\cobarlurie:=\Pi_{2,*}^{(S)}\ \widetilde{\Pi_{1}^{*}}

the
derived (Lurie) cobar construction
if
\Pi_{2,*}^{(S)}
(relative right Kan extension, cf.
2.66
) exists on the image of
\widetilde{\Pi_{1}^{*}}
.

It is clear from Corollary
3.6
and
[
41
, Theorem 5.2.2.17]
that for
I=\mathcal{S}=\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}
these are precisely the (co)bar constructions defined by Lurie in
[
41
, Definition 5.2.2.1]
.

Remark 3.8
.

The above discussion already makes sense, if
\mathcal{S}
is a usual
\infty
-category — considered as a trivial
\infty
-operad with only 1-ary morphism spaces — or even if
\mathcal{S}=\cdot
is the terminal category. In the latter case
\mathcal{C}
is just an
\infty
-category and
I
a diagram (small
\infty
-category). Then the discussion boils down to the following statement: The diagram

\textstyle{({}^{\downarrow\uparrow}\mathcal{C})^{I}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}^{I}}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{C}^{\op})^{I}}

(28)

defines a pairing classified by

(C,D)\mapsto\Hom_{\mathcal{C}^{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I}}(\pi_{2}^{*}C,\pi_{1}^{*}D)\cong\Hom_{\mathcal{C}^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I}}(\pi_{1}^{*}C,\pi_{2}^{*}D).

Thus this pairing is always left and right representable, i.e. the derived bar and cobar constructions exist, if
\pi_{2}^{*}
or
\pi_{1}^{*}
has a right adjoint resp. left adjoint, i.e. if
\mathcal{C}
has the relevant limits and colimits.

Exercise 3.9
.

For
I=\lefthalfcap
the pairing (
28
) is perfect if and only if
\mathcal{C}
is stable.

Proof of Proposition
3.3
.

By the discussion in
3.4
, and Corollary
2.65
, the diagram (
26
) is identified with

\textstyle{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}((\mathcal{C}^{\vee})_{\pi_{2}^{*}S^{\op}}^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I})^{(1,2),(2,1)-\cart}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{1}}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})_{\pi_{2}^{*}S^{\op}}^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I,2-\cart}}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{C}_{\pi_{2}^{*}S^{\op}}^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I,1-\cart})^{\op}}

where the functors are induced by the projections
\rho_{1}
and
\rho_{2}
, and where

{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}(\mathcal{C}_{\pi_{2}^{*}S^{\op}}^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I})^{(1,2),(2,1)-\cart}\subseteq{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}(\mathcal{C}_{\pi_{2}^{*}S}^{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I})

is precisely the full-subcategory of the objects that map via
\rho_{1}
and
\rho_{2}
to the
full subcategories in the diagram. One sees immediately that
\rho_{1}\times\rho_{2}
is, as full subcategory of the twisted arrow category, a fibration with fiber over
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
in
{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}(\mathcal{C}_{\pi_{2}^{*}S^{\op}}^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I})
being the groupoid

\Hom_{\mathcal{C}_{\pi_{2}^{*}S^{\op}}^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I}}(\mathcal{E},\pi_{2}^{*}\mathcal{F})

where
\mathcal{E}
is considered via
\widetilde{\pi_{1}^{*}}
as an object in
\mathcal{C}^{I}_{S}\cong\mathcal{C}_{\pi_{2}^{*}S^{\op}}^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I,2-\cart}
and
\mathcal{F}
an object in
(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})_{S^{\op}}^{I^{\op}}
.
The other statement is obtained dually by replacing
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{S}
by
(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{\op}\to\mathcal{S}
.
∎

3.2
The derived (co)bar for (co)algebras

In this section we discuss the derived (co)bar for plain (co)algebras i.e. the case
I=\mathcal{S}=\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}
. In particular, we establish easy criteria for the derived (Lurie’s) (co)bar functors to exist.
This gives an alternative (to
[
41
]
) approach to the proof. For some statements concerning basic 1-operads appearing, which are purely combinatorial, we refer to the subsequent Chapter
4
where these fact are discussed thoroughly.

Hence let
I=\mathcal{S}=\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}
be the associative planar operad and
S=\id
. In this case, a cofibration
\mathcal{C}\to\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}
is the same thing as a monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
and

\mathcal{C}^{I}_{S}\cong\Alg(\mathcal{C})\qquad(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{I^{\op}}_{S^{\op}}\cong\Coalg(\mathcal{C})

The following is clear by construction (cf. also
4.10
):

Lemma 3.10
.

There is a canonical isomorphism of 1-cooperads (cf.
2.20
)

{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}=(\Delta_{\act}^{\op},\ast^{\prime})^{\vee}

where
\ast^{\prime}
is concatenation, identifying the extremal points.

The bar diagram (
27
, RHS) has the following form in this case:

.
\textstyle{(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{(\Delta_{\act}^{\op},*^{\prime})^{\op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\pi_{1}^{*}}}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{2}^{*}}
\textstyle{\Alg(\mathcal{C})}
\textstyle{\Coalg(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})}

If the fibers of
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{S}
have finite limits and
\otimes
commutes with them, we have

(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{(\Delta_{\act}^{\op},*^{\prime})^{\op}}\cong D((\Delta_{\act},\ast^{\prime})^{\op},\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}^{\op}}=\Coalg(\mathcal{C}^{\Delta_{\act}^{\op}}).

where
D((\Delta_{\act},\ast^{\prime})^{\op},\mathcal{C}^{\vee})
is the Day convolution coalgebra (Proposition
2.30
) which, in this case is
\mathcal{C}^{\Delta_{\act}^{\op}}
equipped with the new monoidal product

(A,B)\mapsto(\ast^{\prime})_{*}A\boxtimes B

considered as cooperad
(cf. Proposition
2.37
).

\widetilde{\pi_{1}^{*}}
is a functor which maps an algebra
A
to a coalgebra in
\mathcal{C}^{\Delta_{\act}^{\op}}
of the form (not depicting all but one degeneracy):

\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\otimes A\otimes A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\otimes A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces 1}

in which the non-degenerate morphisms are given by the multiplication in
A
and the degeneracies by inserting units.

By applying Proposition
2.76
,
\pi_{2}^{*}
has a left adjoint, i.e. a relative (operadic) left Kan existension
\pi_{2,!}^{(S^{\op})}
as soon as
\mathcal{C}
is
L
-admissible.
However, we do not want to assume the commutation of
\otimes
with any colimits, and this is also too general, because we did not discuss augmentations so far
20
20

20

We ignore whether it would make sense to consider this in the absence of augmentations. It amounts probably to applying the augmented construction that we are about to discuss to a freely (co)augmented (co)algebra.
.
Notice that it is not reasonable to expect that
\pi_{2}^{*}
has a relative left Kan existension
\pi_{2,!}^{(S^{\op})}
that is computed fiber-wise (i.e. here compatible with the
forgetful functors forgetting the coalgebra structure, i.e. given as the colimit of the underlying diagram of shape
\Delta_{\act}^{\op}
). By Corollary
2.69
(cf. also Remark
2.71
), a sufficient criterion would be that
\ast^{\prime}:\Delta_{\act}^{\op}\times\Delta_{\act}^{\op}\to\Delta_{\act}^{\op}
is
\infty
-cofinal, which is not true. Notice also that the colimit of the underlying diagram of shape
\Delta_{\act}^{\op}
would be just evaluation at
[1]
because that is a final object in
\Delta_{\act}^{\op}
, and
A
does not carry a coalgebra structure in general.

3.11.

For the remaining part of the section we assume that
\mathcal{C}
is a monoidal
\infty
-category admitting geometric realizations, and that
in
\mathcal{C}
the unit
1
is a final object
. Notice: This can always be achieved by considering augmented objects in
\mathcal{C}
(cf.
[
41
, 5.2.3.9]
). We do
not
assume any compatibility of
\otimes
with geometric realizations (no
L
-admissibility).

Theorem 3.12
.

1.

Under assumption
3.11

\rho^{*}:(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{(\Delta,\ast)^{\op}}\to(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{(\Delta_{\act},\ast^{\prime})^{\op}}

(cf.
4.12
) is an isomorphism.

2.

Furthermore, a fiber-wise relative left Kan extension

\colim_{\Delta^{\op}}:(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{(\Delta,\ast)^{\op}}\to\Coalg(\mathcal{C})

exists, and hence Lurie’s bar construction
\barlurie=\colim_{\Delta^{\op}}\circ(\rho^{*})^{-1}\circ\widetilde{\pi_{1}^{*}}
exists.

Proof.

1. is Corollary
4.14
.
2. follows from Corollary
2.69
,
because a)
\mathcal{C}^{\vee}\to\mathcal{S}^{\op}
is, as fibration of cooperads, automatically
L
-admissible (and geometric realization is the only colimit needed)
and b) by Lemma
4.8
,
(\Delta,\ast)^{\op}\to\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}^{\op}
is an
\infty
-exponential fibration of cooperads (it is almost monoidal w.r.t. the product
\ast
, i.e. a fibration of cooperads, but lacks counits) and the morphism is
\infty
-coCartesian (this amount to
\dec=\ast:\Delta^{\op}\times\Delta^{\op}\to\Delta^{\op}
being
\infty
-cofinal).
∎

Corollary 3.13

(Lurie
[
41
, Theorem 5.2.2.17]
)
.

If
\mathcal{C}
is a monoidal
\infty
-category such that 1 is final and initial and which admits geometric realizations, and such that
\mathcal{C}^{\op}
admits geometric realizations, then Lurie’s bar and cobar constructions exist and form an adjunction

\textstyle{\Alg(\mathcal{C})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\barlurie}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\Coalg(\mathcal{C})}
\scriptstyle{\cobarlurie}

with
\barlurie
left adjoint.

3.14.

Under assumption
3.11

Lurie’s Bar is thus given by the composition:

\textstyle{\Alg(\mathcal{C})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\barconst=\widetilde{\pi_{1}^{*}}}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{(\Delta_{\act},\ast^{\prime})^{\op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\rho^{*})^{-1}}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{(\Delta,\ast)^{\op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\colim_{\Delta^{\op}}}
\textstyle{\Coalg(\mathcal{C})}

The composition
(\rho^{*})^{-1}\circ\widetilde{\pi_{1}^{*}}
maps an algebra
A
to a coalgebra in
\mathcal{C}^{\Delta^{\op}}
of the form (not depicting degeneracies):

\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\otimes A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1}

in which the non-degenerate active morphisms are given by the multiplication in
A
, the inert morphisms by the (canonical) augmentations, and the degeneracies are given by inserting units. This will be discussed in more detail in Section
3.4
.

3.3
General classical cobar — existence

Theorem 3.15
.

Assume that
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{S}
is an
L
-admissible cofibration of operads (i.e. has cocomplete fibers and the push-forward functors respect colimits in each variable separately)
then for each small
\infty
-operad
I
and
S\in\mathcal{S}^{I}
, the classical bar functor (Definition
3.5
)

\barconst=\widetilde{\pi_{1}^{*}}:\mathcal{C}_{S}^{I}\to(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})_{\pi_{2}^{*}S^{\op}}^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I}

has a left adjoint
\cobarconst
(classical cobar construction) given by the restriction of the relative left Kan extension

\pi_{3,!}^{(S)}:\mathcal{C}_{\pi_{3}^{*}S}^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow}I,3-\cocart}\to\mathcal{C}_{S}^{I}.

The
\infty
-categories
(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})_{\pi_{2}^{*}S^{\op}}^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I}
and
\mathcal{C}_{\pi_{3}^{*}S}^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow}I,3-\cocart}
are canonically equivalent.

We stated the Theorem in its most simple form. It is easy to extract from the proof, exactly which colimits need to exist and have to be preserved by the push-forward functors.

Note that
\pi_{3}:{}^{\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow}I\to I
is a cofibration and thus the Kan extension
\pi_{3,!}^{(S)}
exists by Corollary
2.69
(assuming
L
-admissibility) and is computed fiber-wise. For
I=\mathcal{S}=\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}
, for instance, where there is only one fiber, is is given by a colimit
over
{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}\Delta_{\act}^{\op}
which is essentially the category of necklaces (
4.10
). See Section
3.4
for a thorough discussion of classical cobar for
I=\mathcal{S}=\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}
.

There is a morphism
\widetilde{\pi_{1}}:({}^{\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow}I,\pi_{3}^{*}S)\to(I,S)
in
\Dia(\mathcal{S})
(cf.
2.73
) given by the obvious diagram

\textstyle{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow}I\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{3}^{*}S}
\scriptstyle{\Downarrow^{S(\mu)}}
\textstyle{I\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{S}
\textstyle{\mathcal{S}}

Be aware that
\pi_{1}
is only a generalized morphism of
\infty
-operads (in the sense of Definition
2.72
). Nevertheless,
\widetilde{\pi_{1}}
induces a pull-back functor

\widetilde{\pi_{1}}^{*}=S(\mu)_{\bullet}\pi_{1}^{*}:\mathcal{C}_{S}^{I}\to\mathcal{C}_{\pi_{3}^{*}S}^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow}I}

as explained in
2.74
.

Lemma 3.16
.

We have a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{S}^{I}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\pi_{1}^{*}}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\pi_{1}}^{*}=S(\mu)_{\bullet}\pi_{1}^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\pi_{3}^{*}S}^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow}I,3-\cocart}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})_{\pi_{2}^{*}S^{\op}}^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\pi_{12}^{*}}}
\textstyle{\Hom^{\lax,\inert,3-\pseudo}_{(\Cat_{\infty}^{\times})^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow}I}}(\cdot,\pi_{3}^{*}\Xi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{234}^{*}}
\textstyle{\Hom^{\lax,\inert-\pseudo}_{(\Cat_{\infty}^{\times})^{I}}(\cdot,\Xi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Hom^{\oplax,\inert-\pseudo}_{(\Cat_{\infty}^{\times})^{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I}}(\cdot,\pi_{2}^{*}\Xi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\Hom^{\lax,\inert,1,4-\pseudo}_{(\Cat_{\infty}^{\times})^{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow}I}}(\cdot,\pi_{4}^{*}\Xi)}

where
\Xi:I\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\to\Cat_{\infty}
is the morphism classifying the pull-back of the cofibration
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{S}
via
S
,
and where the lower horizontal morphisms are given by Proposition
2.58
using the obvious isomorphism
{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}({}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I)={}^{\uparrow\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow}I
. The dashed morphism is defined by this diagram.

Proof.

Follows from Propositions
2.54
and
2.58
examining the construction. For the fact that
\pi_{234}^{*}
is an isomorphism use Lemma

and reason as in the proof of Lemma

.
∎

Lemma 3.17
.

Let
I
be a small
\infty
-operad.
Consider the
\infty
-coCartesian
21
21

21

Because both are cofibrations this means just: it maps coCartesian to coCartesian morphisms
functor

of cofibrations over

).
It has a transpose over

(which, by construction, can be identified with the functor of cooperads

).

1.

The functor

\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\pi_{3}^{*}S}^{{}^{\downarrow\downarrow}I}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{13}^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\pi_{3}^{*}S}^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow}I}}

has a left adjoint
\pi_{13,!}^{(\pi_{3}^{*}S)}
(i.e. a relative left Kan extension).

2.

An object
\mathcal{E}\in\mathcal{C}^{K}_{\pi_{3}^{*}S}
for either category

is 3-Cartesian
22
22

22

i.e. type-3 morphisms (choosing the same indexing in both categories) in

are mapped to Cartesian morphisms
if and only if for all active
\alpha:i^{\prime}\to i
in
