\alpha_{\mathcal{C},\bullet}(\iota_{i^{\prime}})^{*}\mathcal{E}\to(\alpha_{\bullet,K})^{*}(\iota_{i})^{*}\mathcal{E}

is an isomorphism where
\iota_{i^{\prime}}:K_{i^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow K
, resp.
\iota_{i}:K_{i}\hookrightarrow K
are the inclusions of the respective fibers.

3.

The functor
\pi_{13}^{\vee}
is
\infty
-coCartesian (Definition
2.41
) as well, i.e. for all active
\alpha:i^{\prime}\to i
in
I

\alpha_{\bullet,{}^{\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow}I}\,{}^{t}\!\pi_{13}\cong\,{}^{t}\!\pi_{13}\,\alpha_{\bullet,{}^{\downarrow\downarrow}I}.

Notice
\alpha_{\bullet,{}^{\downarrow\downarrow}I}=\alpha^{\bullet}_{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I}
and similarly for
{}^{\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow}I
.

4.

The functor
\pi_{13,!}^{(\pi_{3}^{*}S)}
preserves 3-coCartesian objects.

Proof.

1. follows from Corollary
2.69
.

2. follows from the definitions.

3. Consider an active morphism
\alpha:i\to i^{\prime}
in
I
. The assertion is the statement that the commutative diagram

\textstyle{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I\times_{/I}i\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{\bullet,{}^{\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow}I}}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{13}}
\textstyle{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I\times_{/I}i^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{13}}
\textstyle{I\times_{/I}i\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{\bullet,{}^{\downarrow\downarrow}I}}
\textstyle{I\times_{/I}i^{\prime}}

(29)

be exact. By Lemma
2.8
, 4., this is the case, if for all objects
i_{0}\to i_{1}\to i^{\prime}
of
{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I\times_{/I}i^{\prime}
and objects
i_{2}\to i
of
I\times_{/I}i
the morphism

\{i_{0}\to i_{1}\to i^{\prime}\}\times_{/{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}(I\times_{/I}i^{\prime})}{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}(I\times_{/I}i)\times_{/(I\times_{/I}i)}\{i_{2}\to i\}\to\Hom_{I\times_{I}i^{\prime}}(i_{0}\to i^{\prime},i_{2}\to i^{\prime})

is an equivalence after forming groupoids. The left hand side is a category of diagrams in
I
of the form

\textstyle{i_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{x\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{i_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{i_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{i\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{i^{\prime}}

contravariant in
x
and covariant in
y
. There is chain of adjunctions to the category of diagrams of the form

\textstyle{i_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{i_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{i_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{i_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{i_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{i\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{i^{\prime}}

which is equivalent to
\Hom_{I\times_{/I}i^{\prime}}(i_{0}\to i^{\prime},i_{2}\to i^{\prime})
. Therefore the diagram (
29
) is exact.

4. Let
\mathcal{E}
be a 3-coCartesian object.
Using 2., we have to prove that

\alpha_{\mathcal{C},\bullet}(\iota_{i^{\prime}})^{*}\pi_{13,!}\mathcal{E}\to(\alpha_{\bullet,K})^{*}(\iota_{i})^{*}\pi_{13,!}\mathcal{E}

is an isomorphism. Since
\pi_{13,!}
is computed fiber-wise (w.r.t.
\pi_{3}
) this is the same as

\alpha_{\mathcal{C},\bullet}\pi_{13,!}(\iota_{i^{\prime}})^{*}\mathcal{E}\to(\alpha_{\bullet,K})^{*}\pi_{13,!}(\iota_{i})^{*}\mathcal{E}.

Examining the proof of Proposition
2.68
we have a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{\alpha_{\mathcal{C},\bullet}\pi_{13,!}(\iota_{i^{\prime}})^{*}\mathcal{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\alpha_{\bullet,K})^{*}\pi_{13,!}(\iota_{i})^{*}\mathcal{E}}
\textstyle{\pi_{13,!}\alpha_{\mathcal{C},\bullet}(\iota_{i^{\prime}})^{*}\mathcal{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\pi_{13,!}(\alpha_{\bullet,K})^{*}(\iota_{i})^{*}\mathcal{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

where the lower morphism is
\pi_{13,!}
applied to the isomorphism expressing the 3-coCartesianity of
\mathcal{E}
and the vertical maps are the obvious mates.
The left hand side morphism is an isomorphism because of the
L
-admissibility of
\mathcal{C}
and the right hand side is an isomorphism by 3. Therefore
\pi_{13,!}^{(\pi_{3}^{*}S)}\mathcal{E}
is 3-coCartesian.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3.15
.

By Lemma
3.16

the functor
\barconst=\widetilde{\pi_{1}^{*}}
is isomorphic to the functor

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 9.50764pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-9.50764pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{S}^{I}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.84865pt\raise 7.48611pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.73611pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\pi_{1}}^{*}=S(\mu)_{\bullet}\pi_{1}^{*}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 63.50764pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 33.50764pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 63.50764pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\pi_{3}^{*}S}^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow}I,3-\cocart}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

It is not reasonable to expect that
\widetilde{\pi_{1}}^{*}
has a relative left Kan extension on all of
\mathcal{C}_{\pi_{3}^{*}X}^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow}I}
(notice that
\widetilde{\pi_{1}}
is not of diagram type).
In the statement of the Theorem it is claimed that the relative left Kan extension
\pi_{3,!}^{(S)}
is left adjoint to
\widetilde{\pi_{1}}^{*}

when restricted to the full subcategory of 3-coCartesian objects.

Observe that the functor in question factors:

\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{S}^{I}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\pi_{1}}^{*}=S(\mu)_{\bullet}\pi_{1}^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\pi_{3}^{*}S}^{{}^{\downarrow\downarrow}I}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{13}^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\pi_{3}^{*}S}^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow}I}}

where we have numbered the indices in
{}^{\downarrow\downarrow}I
and
{}^{\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow}I
in a coherent way.
The left functor is an equivalence onto the subcategory of 3-coCartesian objects of the category in the middle and
\widetilde{\pi_{1}}^{*}\pi_{3,!}=\widetilde{\pi_{1}}^{*}\delta^{*}=\pi_{1}^{*}\delta^{*}
is, in fact, a
right
coCartesian projector, or in other words,
\pi_{3,!}
is a
right
adjoint to the fully-faithful inclusion
\widetilde{\pi_{1}}^{*}
.
The relative left Kan extension
\pi_{13,!}^{(\pi_{3}^{*}S)}
exists and is computed fiber-wise over
\pi_{3}
by Lemma
3.17
, 1.
It thus suffices to see that
\pi_{13,!}^{(\pi_{3}^{*}S)}
preserves the condition of being 3-coCartesian. This is Lemma
3.17
, 4.
∎

3.4
Classical cobar for coalgebras

In this section, we discuss the classical cobar construction (Definition
3.5
) associated with the operad
\mathcal{O}
for
arbitrary monoidal (
\infty
-)categories
,
i.e. letting
(\mathcal{C},\otimes)\to\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}
a monoidal (
\infty
-)category (considered as cofibration of operads) and
I:=\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}
and
S:=\id
in the abstract setting (
3.1
).

Assume that
\mathcal{C}
is countably cocomplete and such that
\otimes
commutes with countable colimits. Assume also that
\mathcal{C}
is finitely complete, and such
\otimes
commutes with
\dec_{*}
(in the Abelian 1-categorical case this means only that
\otimes
has to commute with finite products which we always assume anyway).

Recall the abstract classical (co)bar adjunction:

\textstyle{((\mathcal{C},\otimes)^{\vee})^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cobarconst}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces(\mathcal{C},\otimes)^{\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}}.}
\scriptstyle{\barconst}

We will always apply
\cobarconst
to an object in the image of
\rho^{*}
:

\textstyle{((\mathcal{C},\otimes)^{\vee})^{(\Delta,*)^{\op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{*}}
\textstyle{((\mathcal{C},\otimes)^{\vee})^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}}}

(cf.
4.16
and
4.12
).

3.18.

The source of
\rho^{*}
is just
\Coalg(\mathcal{C}^{\Delta^{\op}},\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})
under suitable
R
-admissibility.
If
\mathcal{C}
is an Abelian 1-category, an object in
\Coalg(\mathcal{C}^{\Delta^{\op}},\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})
is thus a coalgebra in non-negatively graded complexes (a dg-coalgebra) w.r.t. to the usual tensor product of complexes.
In
((\mathcal{C},\otimes)^{\vee})^{(\Delta,*)^{\op}}
it translates to an object
A\in\mathcal{C}^{\Delta^{\op}}
with comultiplication and counit

\mu_{n,m}:A_{[n]\ast[m]}\to A_{[n]}\otimes A_{[m]}\qquad A_{[n]}\to 1

in such a way that

\textstyle{A_{[n]\ast^{\prime}[m]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s_{\can}}
\textstyle{A_{[n]\ast[m]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{n,m}}
\textstyle{A_{[n]}\otimes A_{[m]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{n+m}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{m_{n,m}}
\textstyle{A_{n}\otimes A_{m}}

is commutative, where in the bottom row,
m_{n,m}
is the component of the comultiplication in the complexes viewpoint (cf. Proposition
5.39
).

Back in the general case, pulling back under the morphism
\rho:{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}=(\Delta^{\op}_{\act},*^{\prime})\to(\Delta,*)^{\op}
, we get an object
\rho^{*}A
in

((\mathcal{C},\otimes)^{\vee})^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}{\cal O}}
, i.e. the same
A_{[n]}
with structure maps

\mu_{n,m}^{\prime}:A_{[n]\ast^{\prime}[m]}\to A_{[n]}\otimes A_{[m]}\qquad A_{[0]}\to 1

which are nothing else then the composition with the canonical degeneracy (considered before in the Abelian case) and
the counit is the corresponding restriction to
A_{[0]}
. If 1 is final in
\mathcal{D}
, Lemma
4.13
below implies, that
A
can be recovered from
\rho^{*}A
.

To compute
\cobarconst\rho^{*}A
using Theorem
3.15
, we have to identify

((\mathcal{C},\otimes)^{\vee})^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}{\cal O}}\cong(\mathcal{C},\otimes)^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow}{\cal O},3-\cocart}

and then take the relative Kan extension
\pi_{3,!}
. See
4.10
below for a more thorough discussion of
{}^{\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow}{\cal O}
which is essentially the category of necklaces cf.
[
20
,
50
,
7
]
.

Since
\pi_{3}
is a cofibration this is computed fiber-wise (Corollary
2.69
) and thus its underlying object in
\mathcal{C}
is given by a colimit over the category

\mathcal{C}^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow}{\cal O}}_{[1]}=\mathcal{C}^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}\Delta^{\op}_{\act}}
.

\rho^{*}A
corresponds to the following object
B\in\mathcal{C}^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}\Delta^{\op}_{\act}}:

Active morphisms are mapped

[n]\leftarrow[m]\quad\mapsto\quad A_{[n_{1}]}\otimes\cdots\otimes A_{[n_{m}]}

where
[n]=[n_{1}]\ast^{\prime}\cdots\ast^{\prime}[n_{m}]
is the induced decomposition of
[n]
(which corresponds to taking fibers identifying
\Delta^{\op}_{\act}\cong\Delta_{\emptyset}
via Lemma
4.9
).
A type-1 morphism

\textstyle{{[n]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{[m]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{[n^{\prime}]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{[m]}}

maps to the factor-wise structure morphism

A_{[n_{1}]}\otimes\cdots\otimes A_{[n_{m}]}\to A_{[n_{1}^{\prime}]}\otimes\cdots\otimes A_{[n_{m}^{\prime}]}

while a type-2 morphism corresponds to applying the structure morphism of the algebra, for instance

\textstyle{{[n_{1}]\ast^{\prime}[n_{2}]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{[1]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{1}}
\textstyle{{[n_{1}]\ast^{\prime}[n_{2}]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{[2]}}

maps to the component of the comultiplication:

\mu_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{\prime}:A_{[n_{1}]}\otimes A_{[n_{2}]}\to A_{[n_{1}]\ast^{\prime}[n_{2}]}.

3.19.

The colimit over
{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}\Delta^{\op}_{\act}
can be computed using the observation
2.11
.
If
\mathcal{C}
is a 1-category then this colimit is very simple: In
2.11
it suffices to restrict to the
cokernel of the last two maps and we may restrict to a generating set of morphisms and get:

\displaystyle\boxed{\cobarconst\rho^{*}A=\colim\left(\begin{subarray}{c}\coprod A_{[1]}\otimes\cdots\times A_{[2]}\otimes\cdots\otimes A_{[1]}\\
\amalg\\
\coprod A_{[1]}\otimes\cdots\times A_{[0]}\otimes\cdots\otimes A_{[1]}\end{subarray}\rightrightarrows\coprod_{n=0}^{\infty}A_{[1]}^{\otimes n}\right)}

(30)

where the two morphisms are induced by

A_{[2]}\to A_{[1]}\otimes A_{[1]}

(comultiplication) and by

A(\delta_{1}):A_{[2]}\to A_{[1]}

resp.

A_{[0]}\to 1

(counit) and

A(s_{0}):A_{[0]}\to A_{[1]}.

In Chapter
6
this colimit is computed explicitly for a number of mostly 1-categorical examples.

3.5
Functoriality of (co)bar — non-connected (co)bar

3.20.

Let
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{S}
,
\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{S}
be cofibrations of
\infty
-operads and
R:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
be a functor of
\infty
-operads over
\mathcal{S}
.
Assume that
R
has a fiber-wise left adjoint. Then those assemble to a functor of fibrations

Q:\mathcal{D}^{\vee}\to\mathcal{C}^{\vee}

over
\mathcal{S}^{\op}
.
For
S\in\mathcal{S}^{I}
, we get functors

R:\mathcal{C}_{S}^{I}\to\mathcal{D}_{S}^{I}\qquad Q:(\mathcal{D}^{\vee})_{S^{\op}}^{I^{\op}}\to(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})_{S^{\op}}^{I^{\op}}

which are — in terms of the classifying functors
\Xi_{\mathcal{C}},\Xi_{\mathcal{D}}:I\to\mathcal{S}\to(\Cat_{\infty},\times)
given by composition with

R:\Xi_{\mathcal{C}}\Rightarrow\Xi_{\mathcal{D}}
which is a lax morphism:

\Hom^{\lax,\inert-\pseudo}_{(\Cat_{\infty}^{\times})^{I}}(\cdot,\Xi_{\mathcal{C}})\to\Hom^{\lax,\inert-\pseudo}_{(\Cat_{\infty}^{\times})^{I}}(\cdot,\Xi_{\mathcal{D}})

and gives via composition with

Q:\Xi_{\mathcal{D}}\Rightarrow\Xi_{\mathcal{C}}
(its mate), which is an oplax morphism, a morphism

\Hom^{\oplax,\inert-\pseudo}_{(\Cat_{\infty}^{\times})^{I}}(\cdot,\Xi_{\mathcal{D}})\to\Hom^{\oplax,\inert-\pseudo}_{(\Cat_{\infty}^{\times})^{I}}(\cdot,\Xi_{\mathcal{C}}).

3.21.

In this situation we can refine the (co)bar diagrams:

\textstyle{(Q\dashv R)^{\downarrow\uparrow}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{2}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{\op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{S}}

(31)

where
(Q\dashv R)^{\downarrow\uparrow}
is classified by the functor

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\Cat_{\infty}

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{S}

where
\mathcal{X}_{S}
is the pairing defined by the adjunction
Q_{S}\dashv R_{S}
, i.e. classified by

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{S}^{\op}\times\mathcal{C}_{S}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\Cat_{\infty}

\displaystyle D,C

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\Hom(Q(D),C)\cong\Hom(D,R(C))

For each small
\infty
-operad
I
and
S\in\mathcal{S}^{I}
this gives rise to a diagram

\textstyle{((Q\dashv R)^{\downarrow\uparrow})^{I}_{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{2}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}^{I}_{S}}
\textstyle{((\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{I^{\op}}_{S^{\op}})^{\op}}

(32)

3.22.

For
S\in\mathcal{S}^{I}
, there are two natural diagrams

\textstyle{(\mathcal{D}^{\vee})^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I}_{\pi_{2}^{*}S^{\op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\pi_{1}^{*}}}
\scriptstyle{Q}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I}_{\pi_{2}^{*}S^{\op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{2}^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}^{I}_{S}}
\textstyle{((\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{I^{\op}}_{S^{\op}})^{\op}}

\textstyle{\mathcal{D}^{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I}_{\Pi_{2}^{*}S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Pi_{2}^{*}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I}_{\Pi_{2}^{*}S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\Pi_{1}^{*}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}^{I}_{S}}
\textstyle{((\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{I^{\op}}_{S^{\op}})^{\op}}

(33)

We have then similarly:

Proposition 3.23
.

The pairing (
32
) is isomorphic to the pairing defined by the two (isomorphic) groupoid valued functors

\displaystyle(\mathcal{D}^{I}_{S})^{\op}\times(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{I^{\op}}_{S^{\op}}

\displaystyle\to\Gpd_{\infty}

\displaystyle(D,C)

\displaystyle\mapsto\Hom_{(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I}_{\pi_{2}^{*}S^{\op}}}(Q\,\widetilde{\pi_{1}^{*}}D,\pi_{2}^{*}C)\cong\Hom_{\mathcal{C}^{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I}_{\pi_{2}^{*}S}}(\Pi_{2}^{*}D,R\,\widetilde{\Pi_{1}^{*}}C).

The proof is omitted for the moment.

Definition 3.24
.

We call

\barlurie_{Q}:=\pi_{2,!}^{(S^{\op})}\circ Q\circ\widetilde{\pi_{1}^{*}}\qquad\cobarlurie_{R}:=\Pi_{2,!}^{(S)}\circ R\circ\widetilde{\Pi_{1}^{*}}

the derived (co)bar constructions w.r.t. the adjunction
Q\dashv R
, when the respective Kan extensions exist.

As before, the Proposition shows that if
\barlurie_{Q}
and
\cobarlurie_{R}
exist, then the pairing (
32
) is left
and right representable with those functors associated.

For the special case
I=\mathcal{S}=\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}
one can deduce exactly as in Corollary
3.13
:

Corollary 3.25
.

If
\mathcal{D}
is a monoidal
\infty
-category such that 1 is final and which admits geometric realizations, and If
\mathcal{C}
is a monoidal
\infty
-category such that 1 is initial and
such that
\mathcal{C}^{\op}
admits geometric realizations, then the derived bar and cobar constructions w.r.t. the adjunction
Q\dashv R
exist and form an adjunction

\textstyle{\Alg(\mathcal{D})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\barlurie_{Q}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\Coalg(\mathcal{C})}
\scriptstyle{\cobarlurie_{R}}

with
\barlurie_{Q}
left adjoint.

Notice that, if
Q
preserves the monoidal product (not only laxly), then
\widetilde{\pi_{1}^{*}}\circ Q\cong Q\circ\widetilde{\pi_{1}^{*}}
and thus

\barlurie_{Q}=\barlurie\circ Q
and we gain nothing new.

Example 3.26

(Non-connected (co)bar)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a monoidal
\infty
-category such that 1 is final (but not necessarily initial) and let
X\in\Coalg(\mathcal{C})
be a coalgebra.
Consider the following adjunction between monoidal
\infty
-categories:

\textstyle{\mathrm{Bimod}_{X}(\mathcal{C})_{X/}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{Q}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{X/}}
\scriptstyle{R}

Here
\mathrm{Bimod}_{X}(\mathcal{C})
is equipped with the monoidal product
-\otimes_{X}-
described in
[
41
, 4.4.2]
, cf.
[
41
, Proposition 4.4.3.12]
, and unit
X
(considered as
X
-bimodule) and

\mathrm{Bimod}_{X}(\mathcal{C})_{X/}
is, as undercategory under the unit, monoidal itself.
However, also the undercategory
\mathcal{C}_{X/}
is monoidal because
X
is a coalgebra (two morphisms
X\to Y_{1}
and
X\to Y_{2}
are mapped to the
composition
X\to X\otimes X\to Y_{1}\otimes Y_{2}
).

Q
is the forgetful functor which forgets the bimodule structure but keeps the coaugmentation.
R
is the free-bialgebra functor
[
41
, Proposition 4.3.3.12]
transferring the coaugmentation
by means of the adjunction

\Hom_{\mathrm{Bimod}_{X}}(X,RY)\cong\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}(QX,Y)

where
X
is considered as bimodule over itself (= unit in
\mathrm{Bimod}_{X}
).

Then
X
is initial in
\mathrm{Bimod}_{X}(\mathcal{C})_{X/}
and
X\to 1
is final in
\mathcal{C}_{X/}
, hence if
\mathcal{C}^{\op}
has geometric realizations
and
\mathrm{Bimod}_{X}(\mathcal{C})_{X/}
has geometric realizations (it has by the dual of
[
41
, 4.3.3.9]
as soon as
\mathcal{C}
has) then
we have a derived (co)bar adjunction

\textstyle{\Alg(\mathrm{Bimod}_{X}(\mathcal{C})_{X/})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\barlurie_{Q}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\Coalg(\mathcal{C}_{X/})}
\scriptstyle{\cobarlurie_{R}}

Definition 3.27
.

We call
\Alg(\mathrm{Bimod}_{X}(\mathcal{C})_{X/})=\Alg(\mathrm{Bimod}_{X}(\mathcal{C}))
the category of
category objects
of
\mathcal{C}
with objects
X
in this case
23
23

23

I just became aware of the articles
[
44
,
39
]
which contain related theory.
.

See Section
3.6
for the Cartesian case, explaining the notion.

We proceed to discuss a functoriality of the
classical
(co)bar which will be important for comparisons between different such construction (Section
6.10
).

Proposition 3.28
.

There is an adjunction

\cobarconst\circ Q\dashv\barconst\circ R

which is to say there are morphisms

Q\circ\barconst\circ R\to\barconst\qquad\cobarconst\to R\circ\cobarconst\circ Q

satisfying suitable compatibilities.

Proof (sketch).

We have for the fibers

\mathcal{C}^{I}_{S}\cong(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I,2-\cocart}_{\pi_{2}^{*}S^{\op}}

the identification being given by the composition:

\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{S}^{I}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})_{\pi_{2}^{*}S^{\op}}^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I,2-\cocart}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\Hom^{\lax}_{(\Cat_{\infty}^{\times})^{I}}(\cdot,\Xi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\Hom^{\oplax,\inert-\pseudo,2-\pseudo}_{(\Cat_{\infty}^{\times})^{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I}}(\cdot,\pi_{2}^{*}\Xi)}

and similarly dually.
There is a commutative diagram where the natural transformation is given (essentially) by the units of the point-wise adjunction.

\textstyle{\Hom^{\lax}_{(\Cat_{\infty}^{\times})^{I}}(\cdot,\Xi_{\mathcal{D}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\scriptstyle{\Uparrow}
\textstyle{\Hom^{\oplax,\inert-\pseudo}_{(\Cat_{\infty}^{\times})^{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I}}(\cdot,\pi_{2}^{*}\Xi_{\mathcal{D}})}
\textstyle{\Hom^{\lax}_{(\Cat_{\infty}^{\times})^{I}}(\cdot,\Xi_{\mathcal{C}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Hom^{\oplax,\inert-\pseudo}_{(\Cat_{\infty}^{\times})^{{}^{\uparrow\downarrow}I}}(\cdot,\pi_{2}^{*}\Xi_{\mathcal{C}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{Q}

and similarly with (op)lax exchanged.

This defines the 2-morpisms in the following diagrams

\textstyle{\mathcal{C}^{I}_{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Uparrow}
\scriptstyle{R}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I}_{\pi_{2}^{*}S^{\op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\pi_{1}^{*}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}^{I}_{S}}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{D}^{\vee})^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I}_{\pi_{2}^{*}S^{\op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\pi_{1}^{*}}}
\scriptstyle{Q}

and

\textstyle{(\mathcal{C}^{\vee})_{\pi_{2}^{*}S^{\op}}^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}I}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\pi_{12}^{*}}}
\scriptstyle{\Uparrow}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\pi_{3}^{*}S}^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow}I}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{3,!}^{(S)}}
\scriptstyle{R}
\scriptstyle{\Uparrow}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{S}^{I}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{D}^{\vee})_{\pi_{2}^{*}S^{\op}}^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\pi_{12}^{*}}}
\scriptstyle{Q}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}_{\pi_{3}^{*}S}^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow}I}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{3,!}^{(S)}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}_{S}^{I}}

with the morphism
\widetilde{\pi_{12}^{*}}
from Lemma
3.16
.
The proof that these two 2-morphisms yield an adjunction is omitted for the moment.
∎

3.6
Classical and derived (co)bar in the Cartesian case

We consider again the classical and derived (co)bar for
I=\mathcal{S}=\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}
in which case
(\mathcal{C},\otimes)\to\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}
is just a monoidal
\infty
-category.
We investigate the Cartesian case in this section, i.e.
\otimes=\times
.

Recall that if
(\mathcal{C},\times)
is a Cartesian monoidal
\infty
-category then we have

\Alg(\mathcal{C},\times)\cong\mathrm{Mon}(\mathcal{C}),\qquad\Coalg(\mathcal{C},\times)\cong\mathcal{C}.

We will consider the non-connected case (cf.
3.26
) right away.
The connected case can be obtained as the special case
X=\cdot
.
Let
X
be an object of
\mathcal{C}
which we can see as “coalgebra” in this case.
\mathrm{Bimod}_{X}(\mathcal{C})
is, in this case, nothing but

\mathcal{C}_{/X\times X}

with product
M_{1},M_{2}\mapsto M_{1}\times_{\pr_{2},X,\pr_{1}}M_{2}
.
Objects in
\Alg(\mathrm{Bimod}_{X}(\mathcal{C})_{X/})=\Alg(\mathrm{Bimod}_{X}(\mathcal{C}))
are thus
category objects
in
\mathcal{C}
in the usual sense with objects
X
.
In the adjunction

\textstyle{\mathrm{Bimod}_{X}(\mathcal{C})_{X/}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{Q}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{X/}}
\scriptstyle{R}

the functor
Q
is given by forgetting the morphism
M\to X\times X
(but remembering the unit = coaugmentation) and
R
associates with
X\to Y
the “free bimodule”
X\times Y\times X
with obvious augmentation

X\to X\times Y\times X
.

Proposition 3.29
.

We have for
X\to Y
in the category
\mathcal{C}_{X/}

\boxed{\cobarlurie_{R}(X\to Y)=X\times_{Y}X}

with product given by the canonical “concatenation” operation:

(X\times_{Y}X)\times_{X}(X\times_{Y}X)\cong X\times_{Y}X\times_{Y}X\to X\times_{Y}X.

In particular, for
X=\cdot
, we obtain the functor
\mathcal{L}
(loop space).

Consider the morphism
\rho:\Delta\cong\Delta_{\act}^{\op}\subset\Delta^{\op}
(cf.
4.12
). Considering
[n]
as discrete category
\{0,\dots,n\}
,
\rho^{\op}
gives rise to
a fibration
p:\overline{\Delta}\to\Delta
with fiber over
[n]
being
\{0,\dots,n+1\}
.
There is a morphism

\xi:\overline{\Delta}\to\righthalfcup

mapping fiberwise
0
to
(0,1)
,
n+1
to
(1,0)
and everything else to
(1,1)
.

Lemma 3.30
.

The morphism
\xi
is
\infty
-final.

Proof (Sketch) of Proposition
3.29
.

Consider the non-connected cobar diagram (
3.22
) in this case:

\textstyle{ (\mathcal{C}_{X/},\times)^{(\Delta_{\act},\ast^{\prime})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\textstyle{(\mathrm{Bimod}_{X}(\mathcal{C})_{X/},\times_{X})^{(\Delta_{\act},\ast^{\prime})}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{X/}\cong\Coalg(\mathcal{C}_{X/},\times)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\barconst^{\vee}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\Alg(\mathrm{Bimod}_{X}(\mathcal{C})_{X/},\times_{X})}

where
\barconst^{\vee}
is the dual classical bar construction or, in other words the functor
\widetilde{\Pi_{2}^{*}}
from Definition
3.7
.
In the monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Bimod}_{X}(\mathcal{C})_{X/}
, we have (dual of Corollary
4.14
) because the unit
X
is initial:

\rho^{*}:(\mathrm{Bimod}_{X}(\mathcal{C})_{X/})^{(\Delta,\ast)}\cong(\mathrm{Bimod}_{X}(\mathcal{C})_{X/})^{(\Delta_{\act},\ast^{\prime})}

and we have by construction of
\barconst^{\vee}
— for the underlying objects

(\rho^{*})^{-1}\circ R\circ\barconst^{\vee}(X\to Y)=p_{*}\xi^{*}\left(\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 7.53471pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 31.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.06943pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-7.53471pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.05554pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.05554pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Y}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}}\right)

(note that
p
is a discrete fibration and thus
p_{*}\xi^{*}
computes fiber-wise the product
X\times Y^{n}\times X
)
and therefore

\displaystyle\cobarlurie_{R}(X\to Y)=\lim_{\Delta}\circ(\rho^{*})^{-1}\circ R\circ\barconst^{\vee}(X\to Y)

\displaystyle\cong\lim_{\Delta}p_{*}\xi^{*}\left(\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern
