which yield bijections
(\overline{W}X)_{[n]}\cong(\dec_{*}Y)_{[n]}
compatible with the simplicial structure.
∎

Corollary 6.11
.

There is are adjunctions

\textstyle{\mathrm{Grp}^{\Delta^{\op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{Mon}^{\Delta^{\op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{G}
\scriptstyle{\overline{W}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{Set}^{\Delta^{\op}}}
\scriptstyle{M^{\mathrm{Kan}}}
\scriptstyle{G^{\mathrm{Kan}}}

with
\overline{W}
right adjoint.

Proof.

The right hand side is the adjunction
6.6
and the left hand side the group completion adjunction.
∎

Remark 6.12
.

From the non-Abelian Eilenberg-Zilber Theorem
4.65
follows that for a simplicial monoid
X
there is a weak equivalence

\overline{W}X\cong\delta^{*}NX.

6.3
A_{\infty}
-algebras and coalgebras

Let
(\mathcal{C},\otimes)
be an Abelian tensor category.
The classical bar and cobar constructions for
(\mathcal{C},\otimes)
are intimately related to the notions of
A_{\infty}
-algebra and -coalgebra. Hence we will pause the discussion of (co)bar to briefly discuss the latter.
For an extensive introduction we refer to
[
36
]
.

Embed
\mathcal{C}^{\Delta^{\op}}
as
\Ch(\mathcal{C})_{\geq 0}
(via Dold-Kan) into
\Ch(\mathcal{C})
, the category of unbounded complexes which we see here
as
\mathbb{Z}
-graded objects
X
in
\mathcal{C}
with a differential, i.e. a morphism

\mathrm{d}:X\to X

of degree -1 that satisfies
\mathrm{d}^{2}=0
. We define, in the usual way, shift operators

s,s^{-1}:\Ch(\mathcal{C})\to\Ch(\mathcal{C})

with
(sX)_{i}:=X_{i-1}
.
Furthermore there are the adjunctions

\textstyle{ \Ch_{\leq 0}(\mathcal{C})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\Ch(\mathcal{C})}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{\leq 0}}

with
\tau_{\leq 0}
(truncation) right adjoint
and

\textstyle{ \Ch_{\geq 0}(\mathcal{C})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\Ch(\mathcal{C})}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{\geq 0}}

with
\tau_{\geq 0}
(truncation) left adjoint. The inclusions also have the other adjoints which will not be used in the sequel.

We let
\widetilde{\otimes}
be the tensor product
\tot-\boxtimes-
on
\Ch(\mathcal{C})
, i.e. the usual tensor product of complexes.
Motivated by Proposition
5.39
, explicitly normalize
A\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}B

as the following complex

\bigoplus_{i+j=n}A_{i}\otimes B_{j}

with differential

\mathrm{d}=\mathrm{d}_{l}+(-1)^{i}\mathrm{d}_{r}.

(93)

This will hardly ever be an infinite sum in our applications. Other-wise one should of course be more careful about whether to choose the product or coproduct (or a mixture) and assume that
\mathcal{C}
has those.

6.13.

We will always use the
Koszul sign rule
(cf. also the Yoneda product Lemma
5.29
, and how this is related to the realization of complexes as objects in
\mathcal{C}^{\FinSet^{\op}}
). For homogenous morphisms
f:A\to C
,
g:B\to D
we let

f\otimes g:A\otimes B\to C\otimes D
be the morphism, defined on graded pieces as
x\otimes y\mapsto(-1)^{\deg(x)\deg(g)}f(x)\otimes g(y)
and similarly for higher tensors.
For example the differential (
93
) on
A\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}B
is expessed as
1\otimes\dd+\dd\otimes 1
.

6.14.

An algebra object in
\Ch(\mathcal{C})
, i.e. an object in
(\Ch(\mathcal{C}),\widetilde{\otimes})^{\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}}
can be seen as an algebra object in
graded objects such that

\textstyle{A\otimes A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\dd\otimes 1+1\otimes\dd}
\scriptstyle{m}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\dd}
\textstyle{A\otimes A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{m}
\textstyle{A}

commutes (with Koszul sign rule). Similarly for coalgebra objects.
Expressed with elements this reads:

\mathrm{d}(a\cdot b)=(\mathrm{d}a)\cdot b+(-1)^{\deg a}a\cdot(\mathrm{d}b).

6.15.

For
X
a graded object, we let

T^{\coprod}(X)\quad T^{\prod}(X)

be the tensor (co)algebras. To avoid any problems during the abstract discussion for now with infinite direct sums or products, in particular, with their existence and commutation with
\otimes
,
we consider
\mlq T^{\coprod}\mrq(X)\in\Ind-\mathcal{C}
and
\mlq T^{\prod}\mrq(X)\in\Pro-\mathcal{C}
.

\mlq T^{\prod}\mrq(X)
is considered as algebra with

(x_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes x_{i})\boxtimes(x_{i+1}\otimes\cdots\otimes x_{n})\mapsto x_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes x_{n}

\mlq T^{\coprod}\mrq(X)
is considered as coalgebra with the “deconcatenation” coproduct.

x_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes x_{n}\mapsto\sum_{i=0}^{n}(x_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes x_{i})\boxtimes(x_{i+1}\otimes\cdots\otimes x_{n})

Actually, these constructions are precisely dual to each other.

Denote by
\mlq T^{\prod,+}\mrq(X)
the positive part (i.e. without the unit object
1
).

Lemma 6.16
.

1.

Any homomorphism
\dd_{1}:X\to\mlq T^{\prod}\mrq(X)
of degree -1 can be extended uniquely to a morphism

\dd:\mlq T^{\prod}\mrq(X)\to\mlq T^{\prod}\mrq(X)
of degree -1 satisfying the graded Leibniz rule (called a
derivation
).

2.

Any
\alpha_{1}:X\to\mlq T^{\prod,+}\mrq(X)
of degree 0 can be extended uniquely to an algebra homomorphism

\alpha:\mlq T^{\prod}\mrq(X)\to\mlq T^{\prod}\mrq(X).

Proof.

1. The components

\dd_{n}:X^{\otimes n}\to\mlq T^{\prod}\mrq(X)

of the extension
are given by

x_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes x_{n}\mapsto\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1^{\otimes i}\otimes\dd\otimes 1^{\otimes n-i-1})(x_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes x_{n})

with the Koszul sign convention (
6.13
).
To see that this is well-defined, observe that the components

X^{\otimes n}\to X^{\otimes m}
are zero for
n\gg m
.

2. The components

\alpha_{n}:X^{\otimes n}\to\mlq T^{\prod}\mrq(X)

of the extension
are given by

x_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes x_{n}\mapsto\alpha(x_{1})\otimes\cdots\otimes\alpha(x_{n}).

To see that this is well-defined, observe that the components

X^{\otimes n}\to X^{\otimes m}
are zero for
n>m
.
∎

There is a corresponding dual version for the coalgebra
\mlq T^{\coprod}\mrq(X)
.

If
\mathcal{C}
is complete
56
56

56

That
\mathcal{C}
has countable products is enough (and sometimes even no restriction because we work with graded objects) for what we are doing. We leave it to the reader to make the necessary precisions.
, we have a lax monoidal functor

\lim:\Pro-\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}

and thus
\mlq T^{\prod}\mrq(X)
gives rise to an algebra
T^{\prod}:=(\lim\mlq T^{\prod}\mrq)(X)
. Similarly, if
\mathcal{C}
is cocomplete, we have an oplax monoidal functor

\colim:\Ind-\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}

and thus
\mlq T^{\coprod}\mrq(X)
gives rise to a coalgebra
T^{\coprod}:=(\colim\mlq T^{\coprod}\mrq)(X)
.

Definition 6.17
.

Let
(\mathcal{C},\otimes)
be an Abelian tensor category.

1.

A graded object
X
in
\mathcal{C}
together with a differential
\dd
on the graded coalgebra
\mlq T^{\coprod}\mrq(sX)

is called
an
A_{\infty}
-algebra.

2.

Let
X,Y
be
A_{\infty}
-algebras. A
morphism of
A_{\infty}
-algebras
X\to Y
is a morphism of (non-counital) dg-coalgebras
\mlq T^{\coprod,+}\mrq(sX)\to\mlq T^{\coprod,+}\mrq(sY)
.

3.

A graded object
X
in
\mathcal{C}
together with a differential
57
57

57

i.e. a derivation, satisfying
\dd^{2}=0

\dd
on the graded algebra
\mlq T^{\prod}\mrq(s^{-1}X)

is called
an
A_{\infty}
-coalgebra.

4.

Let
X,Y
be
A_{\infty}
-coalgebras. A
morphism of
A_{\infty}
-coalgebras
X\to Y
is a morphism of dg-algebras
\mlq T^{\prod,+}\mrq(s^{-1}X)\to\mlq T^{\prod,+}\mrq(s^{-1}Y)
.

There are notions of (co)units for
A_{\infty}
-(co)algebras which will not be considered here.

6.18.

Translating via Lemma
6.16
, 1., an
A_{\infty}
-algebra is thus determined by a collection
\dd_{i}:sX^{\otimes i}\to sX
of degree -1 satisfying

\sum_{n=r+s+t}\dd_{r+1+t}(1^{\otimes r}\otimes\dd_{s}\otimes 1^{\otimes t})=0.

It is convenient to define maps
m_{i}:X^{\otimes i}\to X
of degree
i-2
by means of the commutative diagram (with Koszul sign rule
6.13
)

\textstyle{(sX)^{\otimes i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\dd_{i}}
\textstyle{sX}
\textstyle{X^{\otimes i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s^{\otimes i}}
\scriptstyle{m_{i}}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s}

where
s
is the canonical map of degree 1
58
58

58

Explicitly
m_{i}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})=(-1)^{\sum(n-i)\deg(x_{i})}s^{-1}\dd_{i}(sx_{1},\dots,sx_{n})
. Equivalently, an
A_{\infty}
-algebra is thus given by a family
m_{i}
, satisfying
the
Stasheff identities
:

\sum_{n=r+s+t}(-1)^{r+st}m_{r+1+t}(1^{\otimes r}\otimes m_{s}\otimes 1^{\otimes t})=0

and components
\alpha_{i}:X^{\otimes i}\to X
give a morphism of
A_{\infty}
-algebras, if and only if

\sum_{n=r+s+t}(-1)^{r+st}\alpha_{r+1+t}(1^{\otimes r}\otimes m_{s}\otimes 1^{\otimes t})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq r\leq n\\
n=i_{1}+\cdots+i_{r}\end{subarray}}(-1)^{s}m_{r}(\alpha_{i_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\alpha_{i_{r}})

with
s=\sum_{j=1}^{r}(r-j)(i_{j}-1)
.

Lemma 6.19
.

There are functors

\displaystyle A_{\infty}:\Alg^{\circ}(\Ch(\mathcal{A}),\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})

\displaystyle\to\Alg^{A_{\infty}}(\Ch(\mathcal{A}),\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})

\displaystyle A_{\infty}:\Coalg^{\circ}(\Ch(\mathcal{A}),\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})

\displaystyle\to\Coalg^{A_{\infty}}(\Ch(\mathcal{A}),\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})

giving as the identity on the underlying graded object and setting for a (co)algebra
(X,m)

m_{1}:=-\dd\qquad m_{2}:=m\qquad m_{j}:=0\ (j\geq 2)

They are faithful, the image on morphisms are precisely those morphisms
\alpha:sX\to T^{\coprod}(sY)
(resp.
\alpha:T^{\prod}(s^{-1}X)\to s^{-1}Y
) that factor through
sX\to sY
(resp.
s^{-1}X\to s^{-1}Y
).
Fixing a graded object
X
, the image is given precisely by those structures on
X
that satisfy
m_{j}=0
for
j\geq 2
.

Proof.

The (co)associativity of
m
is equivalent to the equation
(\mathrm{d}_{2})^{2}=0
and the equation

\mathrm{d}_{1}\mathrm{d}_{2}=-\mathrm{d}_{2}\mathrm{d}_{1}

is equivalent to the compatibility of
m
with the differential.
∎

6.4
The Eilenberg-MacLane bar and Adams cobar construction

6.20.

This section discusses the classical Adams cobar
[
3
]
and Eilenberg-MacLane bar constructions
[
22
]
.
These are
not anymore dual to each other
, i.e. the Eilenberg-MacLane bar construction applied to
\mathcal{C}^{\op}
does not give the Adams cobar construction!
We assume that
\otimes
commutes with (countable) coproducts.
Let
X
be a dg-coalgebra (not necessarily with counit). Then there is a sub-dg-algebra
T^{\oplus}(s^{-1}X)\subset T^{\prod}(s^{-1}X)
whose underlying graded is the free algebra on
s^{-1}X
and the differential is the restriction,
giving a functor

T^{\oplus}(s^{-1}-):\Coalg^{\circ}(\Ch(\mathcal{A}),\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})\to\Alg(\Ch(\mathcal{A}),\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}).

Note that one can get back
T^{\prod}(s^{-1}X)
as a completion of
T^{\oplus}(s^{-1}X)
.

However, observe that the functor
does not extend to
A_{\infty}
-coalgebras and their morphisms
— their data, in contrast to the ones coming from usual dg-coalgebras, do not restrict to
T^{\oplus}(s^{-1}X)
!

Let
X\in\Ch_{\geq 0}(\mathcal{C})
with
\alpha:X\to 1
be an augmented dg-algebra. Denote
\overline{X}:=\ker(\alpha)
and
let
Y\in\Ch_{\geq 0}(\mathcal{C})
with
\beta:1\to Y
be an coaugmented dg-coalgebra. Denote
\overline{Y}=\coker(\beta)
.

\overline{X}
and
\overline{Y}
are again dg-(co)algebras (without (co)unit).

Definition 6.21
.

Assume that (countable) coproducts exist in
\mathcal{C}
.

1.

The coaugmented dg-coalgebra
59
59

59

T^{\coprod}=\colim\mlq T^{\coprod}\mrq
always exists as graded object because it involves only finite coproducts in each degree.

\barconst^{\mathrm{EM}}:=T^{\coprod}(s\overline{X})

with the differential from Lemma
6.19

is called the
Eilenberg-MacLane bar construction
of the augmented algebra
X
.
It comes equipped with a natural coaugmentation given by
1\to T^{\coprod}(s^{-1}\overline{X})
.

2.

Asumme that
\otimes
commutes with countable coproducts.
The augmented dg-algebra

\cobarconst^{\mathrm{Adams}}:=T^{\oplus}(s^{-1}\overline{Y})

with the restriction of the differential from Lemma
6.19
(cf.
6.20
)
is called the
Adams cobar construction
of the coaugmented coalgebra
Y
.
It comes equipped with a natural augmentation given by the projection
T^{\oplus}(s^{-1}\overline{Y})\to 1
.
If
Y
is connected, then it is again an object in
\Ch_{\geq 0}(\mathcal{C})
.

For coalgebras, we have the functor “connected cover”:

P:\Coalg(\Ch(\mathcal{C}),\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})\to\Coalg_{\mathrm{conn}}(\Ch(\mathcal{C})_{\geq 0},\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})

which is left adjoint to the inclusion, where connected means
X_{i}\cong 0
for
i<0
, and that the counit induces an isomorphism
X_{0}\to 1
.
It is defined by

P(X)_{i}=\begin{cases}X_{i}&i>0\\
1&i=0\\
0&i<0\end{cases}

with obvious comultiplication, counit, and differential.

Note that connected coalgebras are
canonically
coaugmented. We will only consider the Adams cobar construction on such connected objects where
s^{-1}\overline{X}
is just

\tau_{\geq 0}(s^{-1}X)
.

6.5
Classical (co)bar for Abelian categories

Let
(\mathcal{C},\otimes)
be an Abelian tensor category.
We will investigate the classical bar construction
\barconst_{(\mathcal{C},\otimes)\to\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}}
and cobar construction
\cobarconst_{(\mathcal{C},\otimes)\to\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}}
(cf.
3.5
).

Lemma 6.22
.

Let
(\mathcal{C},\otimes)
be an Abelian tensor category and
A\in\mathcal{C}
an algebra with augmentation
\alpha:A\to 1
. Denote
\overline{A}:=\ker\alpha
.
Then we have an isomorphism of dg-coalgebras

\textstyle{T^{\coprod}(s\overline{A})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces(\rho^{*})^{-1}\barconst(A)}
\scriptstyle{a-\alpha(a)\cdot 1}

where
\barconst=\barconst_{(\mathcal{C}_{/1},\otimes)\to\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}}
is the classical bar construction (Definition
3.5
) in the category of augmented objects in
\mathcal{C}
and
where
T^{\coprod}(s\overline{A})
(with
\overline{A}
considered as a complex concentrated in degree 0)
is equipped with the differential
m:\overline{A}^{\otimes 2}\to\overline{A}
extended to
T^{\coprod}(s\overline{A})
by means of (the dual of) Lemma
6.16
.

Proof.

The composition

\textstyle{(\mathcal{C}_{/1},\otimes)^{\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\barconst=\widetilde{\pi_{1}^{*}}}
\textstyle{((\mathcal{C}_{/1},\otimes)^{\vee})^{{}^{\downarrow\uparrow}\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces((\mathcal{C}_{/1},\otimes)^{\vee})^{(\Delta,\ast)^{\op}}=\Coalg(\mathcal{C}^{\Delta^{\op}}_{/1},\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}

can be described as follows. The functor
\barconst
maps
A
to object
B
with

B_{[i]}=A^{\otimes i}

with
A^{\otimes 0}=1
understood, with transition morphisms in
\Delta^{\op}_{\act}
given by the corresponding algebra structure maps,
with comultiplication

B_{[i]\ast^{\prime}[j]}\to B_{[i]}\otimes B_{[j]}

being the obvious isomorphism, and with the counit being the isomorphism
B_{[0]}\to 1
. If 1 is final, as it is in the category of augmented objects, then by Corollary
4.14
this extends to an object

(\rho^{*})^{-1}B\in((\mathcal{C},\otimes)^{\vee})^{(\Delta,\ast)^{\op}}=\Coalg(\mathcal{C}^{\Delta^{\op}},\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}),

i.e. to a dg-coalgebra. Inert morphisms go to the corresponding

A^{\otimes i}\to A^{\otimes j}

given by applying the augmentation to the appropriate slots and the comultiplication factors through the canonical degeneracy (this follows from Lemma
4.13
):

B_{[i]\ast^{\prime}[j]}\to B_{[i]\ast[j]}\to B_{[i]}\otimes B_{[j]}

yielding the structure of dg-coalgebra on
\rho^{*}B
.
Let
\overline{A}
be the kernel of the augmentation (considered as graded object in degree 0). Then we have an isomorphisms of dg-coalgebras

\textstyle{T^{\coprod}(s\overline{A})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces(\rho^{*})^{-1}\barconst(A)}
\scriptstyle{a-\alpha(a)\cdot 1}

for one composition is clearly the identity, whereas all tensors of the form
a_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes(\alpha(a_{i})\cdot 1)\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}
are degenerate.
The identification of the differential is left to the reader.
∎

Lemma 6.23
.

Let
(\mathcal{C},\otimes)
be an Abelian tensor category with countable colimits such that
\otimes
commutes with them.
Let
A\in\Coalg(\Ch_{\geq 0}(\mathcal{C}),\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})
be a dg-coalgebra. Then for the classical cobar construction (Definition
3.5
)

\cobarconst=\cobarconst_{(\mathcal{C},\otimes)\to\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}}
:

\cobarconst\rho^{*}A\cong T^{\oplus}(A_{1})/I

where
I
is the ideal generated by
A_{2}
under
m_{1,1}+\mathrm{d}
, where
m_{1,1}:A_{2}\to A_{1}\otimes A_{1}
is the 1,1-component of the comultiplication. The induced algebra structure is given by (the quotient of) the product structure in
T^{\oplus}(A_{1})
. If
A\in((\mathcal{C}_{/1},\otimes)^{\vee})^{(\Delta,*)^{\op}}
is an augmented coalgebra then the same formula is true and the augmentation of the result coincides with the projection
T^{\oplus}(A_{1})/I\to 1
.

Note that the indices refer to the indices under Dold-Kan, i.e.
A_{1}=A_{[1]}/A_{[0]}
and
A_{2}=A_{[2]}/A_{[2]}^{\mathrm{deg}}
.

Proof.

By
3.19
, we have

\displaystyle\cobarconst\rho^{*}A\cong

\displaystyle\coker(\bigoplus A_{[1]}\otimes\cdots\otimes A_{[2]}\otimes\cdots\otimes A_{[1]}\oplus\bigoplus A_{[1]}\otimes\cdots\otimes A_{[0]}\otimes\cdots\otimes A_{[1]}\rightarrow

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{n=0}^{\infty}A_{[1]}^{\otimes n}=T^{\oplus}(A_{[1]}))

with the morphism

\id-\varepsilon:\bigoplus A_{[1]}\otimes\cdots\otimes A_{[0]}\otimes\cdots\otimes A_{[1]}\to T^{\oplus}(A_{[1]})

with
A_{[0]}\subset A_{[1]}
via the (only) degeneracy understood and
\varepsilon:A_{[1]}\to 1
is the counit.
We have an exact sequence (using that
\otimes
commutes with quotients)

\textstyle{\bigoplus A_{[1]}\otimes\cdots\otimes A_{[0]}\otimes\cdots\otimes A_{[1]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T^{\oplus}(A_{[1]})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T(A_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

We have the automorphism

\Omega:T^{\oplus}(A_{[1]})\to T^{\oplus}(A_{[1]})

given on
A_{[1]}
by
a\mapsto a-\varepsilon(a)
. This yields an exact sequence

\textstyle{\bigoplus A_{[1]}\otimes\cdots\otimes A_{[0]}\otimes\cdots\otimes A_{[1]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\id-\varepsilon}
\textstyle{T(A_{[1]})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Omega^{-1}}
\textstyle{T^{\oplus}(A_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

and the ideal
\Omega(J)
coincides with the image of
X
. The morphism

\bigoplus A_{[1]}\otimes\cdots\otimes A_{[2]}\otimes\cdots\otimes A_{[1]}\to T^{\oplus}(A_{[1]})

is induced by the map
A_{[2]}\to T^{\oplus}(A_{[1]})
given by
\mu_{1,1}^{\prime}-\delta_{1}
, where

\mu_{1,1}^{\prime}
is the composition
A_{[2]}\to A_{[3]}\to A_{[1]}\otimes A_{[1]}
(composition of canonical degeneracy and structure map of the coalgebra, cf. the discussion in
3.18
).

We claim that the following is commutative

\textstyle{A_{[2]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{1,1}^{\prime}-\delta_{1}}
\textstyle{T(A_{[1]})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Omega^{-1}}
\textstyle{A_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{m_{1,1}+\mathrm{d}}
\textstyle{T(A_{1})}

The upper composition is given by, writing
\mu(a)=\sum a_{i}\otimes b_{i}
where
a\in A_{[2]}
and
a_{i},b_{i}\in A_{[1]}
:

a\mapsto\mu(a)+\sum\varepsilon(a_{i})b_{i}+\sum\varepsilon(b_{i})a_{i}+\sum\varepsilon(a_{i})\varepsilon(b_{j})-\delta_{1}(a)-\varepsilon(\delta_{1}(a)).

We have
\sum\varepsilon(a_{i})b_{i}=\delta_{0}(a)
and
\sum a_{i}\varepsilon(b_{i})=\delta_{2}(a)
and
\sum\varepsilon(a_{i})\varepsilon(b_{j})=\varepsilon(a)
because of the coalgebra structure. Obviously
\varepsilon(\delta_{1}(a))=\varepsilon(a)
. Hence we are left with

a\mapsto\mu(a)+\mathrm{d}(a)

where
\mathrm{d}
is the alternating face map. In
T(A_{1})
indeed degenerate elements are mapped to 0 and, because of
the commutative diagram

\textstyle{A_{[2]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{1,1}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{A_{[1]}\otimes A_{[1]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{m_{1,1}}
\textstyle{A_{1}\otimes A_{1}}

where
m_{1,1}
in the bottom line is the 1,1-component of the multiplication in
A
considered as coalgebra w.r.t.
\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}
,
the induced map on
A_{2}
is given by
m_{1,1}+\mathrm{d}
.
∎

Theorem 6.24
.

Let
(\mathcal{C},\otimes)
be an Abelian tensor category with countable colimits such that
\otimes
commutes with them.

There is an isomorphism

\boxed{\cobarconst\circ\rho^{*}\cong H_{0}\circ\cobarconst^{\mathrm{Adams}}\circ P}

of functors
60
60

60

the RHS is the category of augmented algebras in
(\Ch_{\geq 0}(\mathcal{C}),\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})
which is the same as algebra objects in augmented objects.

\Coalg(\Ch_{\geq 0}(\mathcal{C}),\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})\to\Alg(\mathcal{C}_{/1},\otimes)

where

\cobarconst=\cobarconst_{(\mathcal{C},\otimes)\to\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}}
is the classical cobar construction (Definition
3.5
), and where
P
is the functor “connected cover” with its canonical coaugmentation.

Proof.

For
A\in\Coalg(\Ch_{\geq 0},\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})
a dg-coalgebra, we have

(H_{0}\circ\cobarconst^{\mathrm{Adams}}\circ P)(A)\cong T^{\oplus}(A_{1})/I,

where
I
is the ideal generated by
\im(\dd+m_{1,1})
. Therefore this follows from Lemma
6.23
.
∎

By definition,
\cobarconst^{\mathrm{Adams}}(PA)=T^{\oplus}(s^{-1}\overline{PA})
,
and
s^{-1}(\overline{PA})
is the same as the truncation
\tau_{\geq 0}(s^{-1}A)=s^{-1}(\tau_{\geq 1}A)
. Furthermore
\tau_{\geq 1}
is oplax monoidal, neglecting the unit, i.e. a functor of cooperads

\tau_{\geq 1}:((\Ch(\mathcal{C}),\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})^{\circ,\vee})\to((\Ch_{\geq 1}(\mathcal{C}),\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})^{\circ,\vee})

hence induces a functor

\tau_{\geq 1}:\Coalg^{\circ}(\Ch(\mathcal{C}),\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})\to\Coalg^{\circ}(\Ch(\mathcal{C}),\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}).

For a morphism
A\to B
of dg-coalgebras, we obtain a commutative diagram of dg-algebras

\textstyle{\mlq T^{\prod}\mrq(s^{-1}A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mlq T^{\prod}\mrq(s^{-1}B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mlq T^{\prod}\mrq(s^{-1}\overline{PA})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mlq T^{\prod}\mrq(s^{-1}\overline{PB})}

More generally, any morphism of dg-algebras
\mlq T^{\prod}\mrq(s^{-1}A)\to\mlq T^{\prod}\mrq(s^{-1}B)
, i.e. in particular an
A_{\infty}
-morphism of (
A_{\infty}
-)coalgebras
A\to B
, induces a unique morphism

\mlq T^{\prod}\mrq(s^{-1}\overline{PA})\to\mlq T^{\prod}\mrq(s^{-1}\overline{PA})
, such that the analogous diagram commutes, but does not necessarily induce a morphism

T^{\oplus}(s^{-1}\overline{PA})\to T^{\oplus}(s^{-1}\overline{PA})
.

We define thus

Definition 6.25
.

Assume that countable products exist in
\mathcal{C}
. Let
A
be a dg-coalgebra with coaugmention
1\to A
.

\widehat{\cobarconst}^{\mathrm{Adams}}(A):=T^{\prod}(s\overline{A})

equipped with its natural algebra structure and with the differential from Lemma
6.19
.

\displaystyle\widehat{\cobarconst}(A):=\coker\left(\begin{subarray}{c}\prod A_{[1]}\otimes\cdots\otimes A_{[2]}\otimes\cdots\otimes A_{[1]}\\
\oplus\\
\prod A_{[1]}\otimes\cdots\otimes A_{[0]}\otimes\cdots\otimes A_{[1]}\end{subarray}\rightarrow\prod_{n=0}^{\infty}A_{[1]}^{\otimes n}\right).

We have then still

Proposition 6.26
.

\widehat{\cobarconst}\rho^{*}A\cong T^{\prod}(A_{1})/\widehat{I}\cong H_{0}\circ\widehat{\cobarconst}^{\mathrm{Adams}}\circ P

where
\widehat{I}
is the closure of the ideal
I
of Lemma
6.23
and this is functorial in morphisms of
A_{\infty}
-coalgebras.

6.6
Classical (co)bar for complexes in Abelian categories

6.27.

Let
(\mathcal{C},\otimes)
be an Abelian tensor category. Apply the last section to
(\Ch_{\geq 0}(\mathcal{C}),\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})
.
Composing with the adjunction
C.4
we get an adjunction

\textstyle{\Alg(\Ch_{\geq 0}(\mathcal{C})_{/1},\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cobarconst\circ\rho^{*}\circ\dec^{*}_{\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\Coalg(\Ch_{\geq 0}(\mathcal{C}),\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})}
\scriptstyle{\dec_{\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}},*}\circ(\rho^{*})^{-1}\circ\barconst}

where
\barconst
and
\cobarconst
are understood w.r.t. the cofibration
(\Ch_{\geq 0}(\mathcal{C}),\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})\to\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}
.
Here
\dec^{*}_{\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}}
is the decalage
\dec^{*}
turned into a lax monoidal functor (=functor of cooperads) as described in Section

.

The adjoint functors will be identified in this section in terms of the Eilenberg-MacLane bar and Adams cobar construction.

Theorem 6.28
.

There is an isomorphism

\boxed{\barconst^{EM}\cong\dec_{\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}},*}\circ(\rho^{*})^{-1}\circ\barconst}

of functors
61
61

61

the LHS is the category of augmented algebras in
(\Ch_{\geq 0}(\mathcal{C}),\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})
which is the same as algebra objects in augmented objects.

\Alg(\Ch_{\geq 0}(\mathcal{C})_{/1},\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})\to\Coalg_{\mathrm{conn}}(\Ch_{\geq 0}(\mathcal{C}),\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})

where
\barconst=\barconst_{(\Ch_{\geq 0}(\mathcal{C})_{/1},\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})\to\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}}
is the classical bar construction (Definition
3.5
)
62
62

62

“
\barconst
” commutes with the forgetful functor forgetting the augmentation (in the obvious sense), hence we didn’t mention the augmentation in the index of “
\barconst
”, but it is important to keep the augmentation along to be a able to interpret the result via
(\rho^{*})^{-1}
as a double complex. (Without the augmentation it is only a diagram of shape
\Delta^{\op}\times\Delta^{\op}_{\act}
).
,
and where
\dec_{*}=\tot
is the total complex functor considered as a monoidal functor as in
C.4
.
Both sides are connected and hence canonically coaugmented coalgebras.

Proof.

Let
A\in\mathcal{C}^{\Delta^{\op}}
be an algebra w.r.t.
\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}
, i.e. a dg-algebra, with augmentation
A\to 1
.
By Lemma
6.22
applied to
(\mathcal{C}^{\Delta^{\op}},\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})
there is an isomorphism

(\rho^{*})^{-1}\barconst(A)=T^{\coprod}_{(\mathcal{C}^{\Delta^{\op}},\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})}(s_{r}\overline{A})

of double complexes (with rows
\overline{A}\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}\cdots\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}\overline{A}
), where
s_{r}
is the shift w.r.t. the second (column) index.
We have to see that there is an isomorphism of dg-algebras:

\dec_{*}T^{\coprod}_{(\mathcal{C}^{\Delta^{\op}},\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})}(s_{r}\overline{A})\cong T^{\coprod}_{(\mathcal{C},\otimes)}(s\overline{A})

where
T^{\coprod}_{(\mathcal{C},\otimes)}
considers
s\overline{A}
as a graded object and is equipped with the differential

\dd_{1}+\dd_{2}

from Lemma
6.19
and where
\dec_{*}
is considered a monoidal functor as described in
C.4
.
Both sides are in a natural way direct sums of
A_{i_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes A_{i_{m}}
in degree
n=m+\sum_{k}i_{k}
.
However, because of the sign in the coalgebra structure on the left hand side (cf.
C.4
), the identity would not be compatible with the coalgebra structures,
but we have an epimorphism of graded objects

p:\dec_{*}T^{\coprod}_{(\mathcal{C}^{\Delta^{\op}},\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})}(s_{l}\overline{A})\to s\overline{A}

by the obvious projection. Like in (the dual of) Lemma
6.16
it extends to an isomorphism

p^{\prime}:\dec_{*}T^{\coprod}_{(\mathcal{C}^{\Delta^{\op}},\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})}(s_{l}\overline{A})\to T^{\coprod}(s\overline{A})

of graded coalgebras, introducing the sign
(-1)^{\sum_{k}(m-k)\deg i_{m-k}}
on the summand
A_{i_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes A_{i_{m}}
.
Finally observe that, for
A_{n}
in complex bidegree
(n,1)
resp. in degree
n+1
, the following commutes:

\textstyle{A_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(-1)^{n}m_{i_{1},i_{2}}}
\textstyle{A_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\dd_{2,i_{1}-1,i_{2}-1}}
\textstyle{A_{i_{1}}\otimes A_{i_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(-1)^{i_{2}}}
\textstyle{A_{i_{1}}\otimes A_{i_{2}}}

where the
(-1)^{n}m_{i_{1},i_{2}}
is the second summand of the differential on the total complex.
∎

6.29.

Let
B\in\Coalg(\Ch_{\geq 0}(\mathcal{C}),\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})

to the double complex

considered as object

.
We have by Proposition

, 2.:

A_{1}=s^{-2}B\oplus s^{-1}B\quad A_{2}=s^{-3}B\oplus s^{-2}B

(not a direct sum as complexes!), where

is considered as endomorphism of

(i.e. really

) and
the differential (between the columns) is

\textstyle{s^{-3}B\oplus s^{-2}B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\left(\begin{matrix}0&1\\
0&0\end{matrix}\right)}
\textstyle{s^{-2}B\oplus s^{-1}B}

and the 1,1-component of the comultiplication is

\textstyle{s^{-3}B\oplus s^{-2}B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(s^{-2}B\oplus s^{-1}B)\otimes(s^{-2}B\oplus s^{-1}B)}

whose components are by Lemma
C.3
, 3. given by:

\displaystyle m_{1,1}:B_{i+j+3}\oplus B_{i+j+2}

\displaystyle\to(B_{i+2}\otimes B_{j+2})\oplus(B_{i+2}\otimes B_{j+1})\oplus(B_{i+1}\otimes B_{j+2})\oplus(B_{i+1}\otimes B_{j+1})

\displaystyle(b_{n+3},b_{n+2})

\displaystyle\mapsto(0,\beta_{i+2,j+1}(b_{n+3}),(-1)^{j+1}\beta_{i+1,j+2}(b_{n+3}),(-1)^{j}\beta_{i+1,j+1}(b_{n+2})).

Theorem 6.30
.

Let

There is an isomorphism

of functors
63
63

63

the RHS is the category of augmented algebras in
(\Ch_{\geq 0}(\mathcal{C}),\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})
which is the same as algebra objects in augmented objects.

\Coalg(\Ch_{\geq 0}(\mathcal{C}),\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})\to\Alg(\Ch_{\geq 0}(\mathcal{C})_{/1},\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})

where

Proposition 6.31
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be an Abelian tensor category with countable colimits such that
\otimes
commutes with them.
In the situation of Lemma
6.23
for
\mathcal{C}^{\Delta^{\op}}
with tensor product
\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}=\dec_{*}-\boxtimes-
(i.e. under Dold-Kan the usual tensor product on complexes) there is an isomorphism of dg-algebras

\displaystyle\phi:T^{\oplus}((\dec^{*}B)_{1})/I

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle T^{\oplus}(s^{-1}B)

\displaystyle b_{n+2},b_{n+1}

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle(-1)^{n}s^{-1}(b_{n+1})+\dd_{2}s^{-1}(b_{n+2})

where
I
is the ideal from Lemma
6.23
and where
T^{\oplus}(s^{-1}B)
is equipped with the restriction of the differential
\dd=\dd_{1}+\dd_{2}
induced by the coalgebra structure on
B
as described in Lemma
6.19
and where
s^{-1}
is the endomorphism of
\Ch(\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0})
(i.e. really
\tau_{\geq 0}s^{-1})
.

Proof.

Recall that
\dd_{1}=-\dd
and
\dd_{2}:s^{-1}B\to s^{-1}B\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}s^{-1}B
is the comultiplication twisted with sign:
\dd_{2,i+1,j+1}=(-1)^{i}\beta_{i,j}
(both extended to
T(s^{-1}B)
using the graded Leibniz rule).
We first check that
\phi
is compatible with the differential and maps
I
to zero. We omit the
s^{-1}
for better readability.

I
is generated by elements of the form

(b_{n+2},0)+\sum_{i+j=n}(0,\beta_{i+2,j+1}(b_{n+3}),(-1)^{j+1}\beta_{i+1,j+2}(b_{n+3}),(-1)^{j}\beta_{i+1,j+1}(b_{n+2}))

which are mapped by
\phi
to

\displaystyle\sum_{i+j=n}\left((-1)^{i+1}\beta_{i+1,j+1}(b_{n+2})+(-1)^{n+j}\beta_{i+1,j+1}(b_{n+2})\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{k+l+m+2=n}(-1)^{m}\left(((-1)^{k+1}\beta_{k+1,l+1}\otimes 1)\beta_{k+l+2,m+1}(b_{n+3})\right.

\displaystyle+\left.(-1)^{k}((-1)^{l+1}1\otimes\beta_{l+1,m+1})(-1)^{l+m+1}\beta_{k+1,l+m+2}(b_{n+3})\right)=0.

We have to check the commutativity of

\textstyle{B_{n+2}\oplus B_{n+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\scriptstyle{\left(\begin{matrix}\dd&(-1)^{n}\\
&\dd\end{matrix}\right)}
\textstyle{T^{\oplus}(s^{-1}B)_{n+2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\dd_{1}+\dd_{2}}
\textstyle{B_{n+1}\oplus B_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{T^{\oplus}(s^{-1}B)_{n+1}}

The upper composition maps a pair
(b_{n+2},b_{n+1})
to

-\dd(-1)^{n}b_{n+1}-\dd\dd_{2}b_{n+2}+\dd_{2}(-1)^{n}b_{n+1}+(\dd_{2})^{2}b_{n+2}.

The lower composition maps it to

(-1)^{n-1}\dd b_{n+1}+\dd_{2}(\dd b_{n+2}+(-1)^{n}b_{n+1}).

This is the same using
(\dd_{2})^{2}=0
and
\dd\dd_{2}=-\dd_{2}\dd
.
Defining

\displaystyle\psi:T^{\oplus}(s^{-1}B)

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle T^{\oplus}((\dec^{*}B)_{1})/I

\displaystyle b_{n+1}

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle(0,(-1)^{n}b_{n+1})

we have
\phi\psi=\id
and

\displaystyle(\psi\phi-\id)(b_{n+2},b_{n+1})=(-b_{n+2},0)+\psi\dd_{2}b_{n+2}

\displaystyle=(-b_{n+2},0)+(-1)^{n}\sum_{i+j=n}\left(0,0,0,(-1)^{i+1}\beta_{i+1,j+1}(b_{n+2})\right)

with the same decomposition in tensor-degree two as above. This is obviously in
I
.
∎

Corollary 6.32
.

Let
(\mathcal{C},\otimes)
be an Abelian tensor category with countable colimits such that
\otimes
commutes with them.
Then there is an adjunction

\textstyle{\Alg(\Ch_{\geq 0}(\mathcal{C})_{/1},\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\barconst^{EM}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\Coalg_{\mathrm{conn}}(\Ch_{\geq 0}(\mathcal{C}),\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}).}
\scriptstyle{\cobarconst^{\mathrm{Adams}}}

Proof.

This is the adjunction
6.27
.
∎

6.7
Comparison of classical and derived (co)bar

As we have seen, the bar constructions
\overline{W}
and
\barconst^{EM}
are given by our abstract “classical bar construction” for
I=\mathcal{S}=\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}
followed by the (totalization) functor
\dec_{*}
.
By definition
64
64

64

Lurie defines them differently, but we have seen in Corollary
3.6
that the definitions agree
the derived bar construction is given by this “classical bar construction”
followed by a relative left Kan extension which (in the augmented case) can be computed as colimit over
\Delta^{\op}
. Thus, simply put,
\overline{W}
and
\barconst^{EM}
give Lurie’s bar construction because

\dec_{*}
computes the (derived) colimit over
\Delta^{\op}
(this is true essentially because of the Eilenberg-Zilber theorem, cf. Section
4.6
). This section is devoted to an elaboration of this explanation.

Denote
\mathrm{S}=\Set^{\Delta^{\op}}[\mathcal{W}^{-1}]\cong\Gpd_{\infty}
(localizations always as
\infty
-categories).

Proposition 6.33
.

We have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{Mon}^{\Delta^{\op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\rho^{*})^{-1}\circ\barconst_{(\Set^{\Delta^{\op}},\times)\to\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}}}
\textstyle{\Alg(\mathrm{S},\times)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\rho^{*})^{-1}\circ\barconst_{(\mathrm{S},\times)\to\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}}}
\textstyle{\Set^{\Delta^{\op}\times\Delta^{\op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\dec_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{S}^{\Delta^{\op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\colim_{\Delta^{\op}}}
\textstyle{\Set^{\Delta^{\op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{S}}

Proof.

The commutation of the upper diagram is clear, and the lower follows from the isomorphism
\dec_{*}\cong\delta^{*}
up to coherent weak equivalences (non-Abelian Eilenberg-Zilber Theorem
4.65
) and the fact that
\delta^{*}
computes the colimit in the localization (i.e. the homotopy colimit) by Proposition
4.51
.
∎

Corollary 6.34
.

We have

\boxed{\barlurie\cong\dec_{*}\circ(\rho^{*})^{-1}\circ\barconst\cong\overline{W}}

as functors

\mathrm{Mon}^{\Delta^{\op}}\to\mathrm{S}

where
\barlurie=\barlurie_{(\mathrm{S},\times)\to\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}}
is Lurie’s Bar construction (Definition
3.7
).

Proof.

We have seen in
3.14
that

\barlurie=\colim_{\Delta^{\op}}\circ(\rho^{*})^{-1}\circ\barconst

(both bar constructions w.r.t. the cofibration
(\mathrm{S},\times)\to\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}
). Therefore this follows from Proposition
6.33
.
∎

6.35.

Let
(\mathcal{C},\otimes)
be an Abelian tensor category.
Denote by
\mathcal{W}
the quasi-isomorphisms in
\Ch_{+}(\mathcal{C})
(bounded below complexes). Assume that
\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}
has
a left derived functor
\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}^{L}
which is again associative.
Denote
\mathcal{D}_{+}(\mathcal{C}):=\Ch_{+}(\mathcal{C})[\mathcal{W}^{-1}]
the localization as
\infty
-category.
We assume the very mild conditions of
[
18
, 7.9.1]
ensuring that localization and taking functor categories commute (i.e. homotopy colimits become
\infty
-categorical colimits). Those conditions are
for example satisfied, if
(\Ch_{+}(\mathcal{C}),\mathcal{W})
enhances to a model category structure. This is true under very mild assumptions on
\mathcal{C}
.

Assume that there is a full subcategory
\Ch_{+}(\mathcal{C})^{\mathrm{split}}\subset\Ch_{+}(\mathcal{C})
(closed under biproducts,
\otimes
, and containing 1) such that the restriction of the inclusion gives rise to a

monoidal
functor

(\Ch_{+}(\mathcal{C})^{\mathrm{split}},\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})\to(\mathcal{D}_{+}(\mathcal{C}),\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}^{L}).

(94)

It thus induces a functor of cooperads

(\Ch_{+}(\mathcal{C})^{\mathrm{split}},\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})^{\vee}\to(\mathcal{D}_{+}(\mathcal{C}),\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}^{L})^{\vee}

and finally a functor

\Coalg(\Ch_{+}(\mathcal{C})^{\mathrm{split}},\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})\to\Coalg(\mathcal{D}_{+}(\mathcal{C}),\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}^{L}).

Proposition 6.36
.

We have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\Alg(\Ch_{+}(\mathcal{C})^{\mathrm{split}}_{/1},\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\rho^{*})^{-1}\circ\barconst_{(\Ch_{+}(\mathcal{C}),\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})\to\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}}}
\textstyle{\Alg(\mathcal{D}_{+}(\mathcal{C})_{/1},\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}^{L})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\rho^{*})^{-1}\circ\barconst_{(\mathcal{D}_{+}(\mathcal{C}),\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}^{L})\to\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}}}
\textstyle{\Coalg((\Ch_{+}(\mathcal{C})^{\mathrm{split}}_{/1})^{\Delta^{\op}},\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\dec_{\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}},*}}
\textstyle{\Coalg(\mathcal{D}_{+}(\mathcal{C})^{\Delta^{\op}}_{/1},\dec_{*}-\mathbin{\tilde{\boxtimes}}^{L}-)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\colim_{\Delta^{\op}}}
\textstyle{\Coalg(\Ch_{+}(\mathcal{C})^{\mathrm{split}}_{/1},\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Coalg(\mathcal{D}_{+}(\mathcal{C})_{/1},\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}^{L})}

Recall from the proof of Theorem
3.12
that the functor denoted
\colim_{\Delta^{\op}}
is in fact a relative left Kan extension along the functor
(\Delta,\ast)^{\op}\to\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}^{\op}
of cooperads, which exists, and is computed fiber-wise (i.e. here commutes with forgetting the coalgebra structure) because this is an exponential fibration of cooperads that is
\infty
-coCartesian.

Proof.

The upper diagram commutes because the restriction of the localization (
94
) is monoidal by assumption.

Consider the diagram

\textstyle{\Coalg(\Ch_{+}(\mathcal{C})^{\mathrm{split}}_{/1},\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\dec_{\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}}^{?}}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{*}}
\textstyle{\Coalg(\Ch_{\geq 0}(\mathcal{C})[\mathcal{W}^{-1}],\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}^{L})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{*}}
\textstyle{\Coalg((\Ch_{+}(\mathcal{C})^{\mathrm{split}}_{/1})^{\Delta^{\op}},\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Coalg(\mathcal{D}_{+}(\mathcal{C})^{\Delta^{\op}},\dec_{*}-\mathbin{\tilde{\boxtimes}}^{L}-)}

in which the square involving the
\pi^{*}
is clearly commutative. Furthermore, also
\dec_{\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}}^{?}
is a functor of cooperads (Lemma
C.10
) and there is a natural transformation

\pi^{*}\Rightarrow\dec_{\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}}^{?}

which is point-wise a quasi-isomorphism (Lemma
C.9
). Therefore there is also a commutative square involving
\dec_{\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}}^{?}
. Now consider the mate (passing to the left adjoints vertically):

\textstyle{\Coalg((\Ch_{+}(\mathcal{C})^{\mathrm{split}}_{/1})^{\Delta^{\op}},\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\dec_{\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}},*}}
\scriptstyle{\Swarrow}
\textstyle{\Coalg(\mathcal{D}_{+}(\mathcal{C})^{\Delta^{\op}},\dec_{*}-\mathbin{\tilde{\boxtimes}}^{L}-)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\colim_{\Delta^{\op}}}
\textstyle{\Coalg(\Ch_{+}(\mathcal{C})^{\mathrm{split}}_{/1},\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Coalg(\mathcal{D}_{+}(\mathcal{C}),\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}^{L})}

We have to show that the natural transformation is an isomorphism. However, this can be shown forgetting the coalgebra structure, i.e. we have to show that

\textstyle{(\Ch_{+}(\mathcal{C})^{\mathrm{split}}_{/1})^{\Delta^{\op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\dec_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\Swarrow}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}_{+}(\mathcal{C})^{\Delta^{\op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\colim_{\Delta^{\op}}}
\textstyle{\Ch_{+}(\mathcal{C})^{\mathrm{split}}_{/1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}_{+}(\mathcal{C})}

commutes. However this is the restriction of

\textstyle{(\Ch_{+}(\mathcal{C})_{/1})^{\Delta^{\op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\dec_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\Swarrow}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}_{+}(\mathcal{C})^{\Delta^{\op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\colim_{\Delta^{\op}}}
\textstyle{\Ch_{+}(\mathcal{C})_{/1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}_{+}(\mathcal{C})}

where the RHS is a localization of the LHS (the first line at the object-wise quasi-isomorphisms, see
[
18
, Proposition 7.9.1]
) and in the adjunction
\dec_{*},\dec^{?}
both functors preserve (object-wise) quasi-isomorphisms.
Therefore it descends to an adjunction between the localizations and (the descended)
\dec^{?}
is isomorphic to
\pi^{*}
— thus also the descended

is isomorphic to

.
∎

Corollary 6.37
.

We have

\boxed{\barlurie\cong\dec_{*}\circ(\rho^{*})^{-1}\circ\barconst\cong\barconst^{EM}}

as functors

\Alg(\Ch_{+}(\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{split}})_{/1},\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})\to\Coalg(\mathcal{D}_{+}(\mathcal{C}),\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}^{L})

where
\barlurie=\barlurie_{(\mathcal{D}_{+}(\mathcal{C})_{/1},\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}^{L})\to\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}}
is Lurie’s Bar construction (Definition
3.7
).

Proof.

We have seen in
3.14
that

\barlurie=\colim_{\Delta^{\op}}\circ(\rho^{*})^{-1}\circ\barconst

(both bar constructions w.r.t. the cofibration
(\mathcal{D}_{+}(\mathcal{C}_{/1},\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}^{L})\to\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}
). Therefore this follows from Proposition
6.36
.
The isomorphism with
\barconst^{EM}
is a slight generalization of Theorem
6.28
to bounded below complexes.
∎

6.8
An intermediate cobar construction

Let
(\mathcal{C},\otimes)
be an Abelian tensor category with countable colimits such that
\otimes
commutes with them.
We may also compute the classical cobar construction w.r.t. the cofibration of operads
(\mathcal{C}^{\Delta^{\op}},\otimes)\to\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}
(not
\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}
!).

(\mathcal{C}^{\Delta^{\op}},\otimes)
is again an Abelian tensor category and thus Lemma
6.23
applies giving

\cobarconst\circ\rho^{*}\circ\dec^{*}_{\otimes}A=T^{\oplus}_{(\mathcal{C}^{\Delta^{\op}},\otimes)}((\dec^{*}A)_{1})/I,

where
\dec_{\otimes}=\mathfrak{AW}_{2}\dec^{*}
is the lax monoidal extension of
\dec^{*}

(\mathcal{C}^{\Delta^{\op}},\otimes)^{\vee}\rightarrow(\mathcal{C}^{\Delta^{\op}\times\Delta^{\op}},\mathbin{\tilde{\underline{\otimes}}})^{\vee}

discussed in
C.3
, where
\mathbin{\tilde{\underline{\otimes}}}:=\dec_{*}-\boxtimes-
, and
\boxtimes
is
\otimes
applied point-wise in the first variable.
We will not calculate this further in any explicit manner, because
\otimes
is complicated in terms of complexes. It will turn up as an
intermediate step in comparing the Adams cobar construction and the geometric cobar construction in section
6.10
.
Actually, it compares very easily to the geometric cobar construction:

Lemma 6.38
.

The following commutes

\textstyle{\Set^{\Delta^{\op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cobarconst\circ\rho^{*}\circ\dec^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{Z}[-]}
\textstyle{\Coalg(\Ab^{\Delta^{\op}},\otimes)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cobarconst_{\otimes}\circ\rho^{*}\circ\dec^{*}_{\otimes}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Mon}^{\Delta^{\op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{Z}[-]}
\textstyle{\Alg(\Ab^{\Delta^{\op}}_{/\mathbb{Z}},\otimes)}

where on the left the cobar construction w.r.t.
(\Set^{\Delta^{\op}},\times)\to\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}
and on the right the cobar construction w.r.t.
(\Ab^{\Delta^{\op}}_{/\mathbb{Z}},\otimes)\to\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}
is used.

Proof.

The diagram in question is the outer rectangle in:

\textstyle{\Set^{\Delta^{\op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\dec^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{Z}[-]}
\textstyle{\Coalg(\Ab^{\Delta^{\op}},\otimes)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\dec^{*}}
\textstyle{\Coalg(\Set^{\Delta^{\op}\times\Delta^{\op}},\times)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{AW}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{Z}[-]}
\textstyle{\Coalg(\Ab^{\Delta^{\op}\times\Delta^{\op}},\otimes)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{AW}_{2}}
\textstyle{\Coalg(\Set^{\Delta^{\op}\times\Delta^{\op}},\mathbin{\tilde{\underline{\times}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cobarconst\circ\rho^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{Z}[-]}
\textstyle{\Coalg(\Ab^{\Delta^{\op}\times\Delta^{\op}},\mathbin{\tilde{\underline{\otimes}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cobarconst_{\otimes}\circ\rho^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Mon}^{\Delta^{\op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{Z}[-]}
\textstyle{\Alg(\Ab^{\Delta^{\op}}_{/\mathbb{Z}},\otimes)}

The left morphism
\mathfrak{AW}_{2}
is an isomorphism because of the trivial Eilenberg-Zilber Theorem
4.70
.
In the third horizontal morphism a coalgebra with structure map
\dec^{*}X\to X\boxtimes X
is mapped to
\dec^{*}\mathbb{Z}[X]
with structure map

\dec^{*}\mathbb{Z}[X]=\mathbb{Z}[\dec^{*}X]\to\mathbb{Z}[X\boxtimes X]=\mathbb{Z}[X]\boxtimes\mathbb{Z}[X]
.
The commutativity of the two upper squares is clear and the commutativity of the lower square stems from the
commutativity of
\mathbb{Z}[-]
with colimits (being a left adjoint) and its monoidality
(\Set^{\Delta^{\op}},\times)\to(\Ab^{\Delta^{\op}},\otimes)
.
∎

Remark 6.39
.

The Lemma may be expressed by saying that there is an isomorphism:

\boxed{\mathbb{Z}[-]\circ M^{\mathrm{Kan}}\cong(\cobarconst_{\otimes}\circ\rho^{*}\circ\dec^{*}_{\otimes})\circ\mathbb{Z}[-]}

i.e. the complex of normalized singular chains of the geometric cobar construction of
X
is the algebraic cobar construction of
the complex of normalized singular chains of
X
. With the caveat that this is the algebraic cobar construction w.r.t.
\otimes
and not
w.r.t.
\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}
(which would be the Adams cobar construction). Nevertheless, the latter two can be compared, which is — perhaps surprisingly —
fairly intricate. We will do this in section
6.10
.

6.9
Coherent vs.
A_{\infty}
-transformations

Proposition 6.40
.

Consider the cooperad
65
65

65

Recall that
(-)^{\circ}
means that we neglect the counits.

(\mathcal{C}^{\Delta^{\op}},\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}})^{\circ,\vee}

Then for any pair of (non-counital) dg-coalgebras

Remark 6.41
.

There is an algebra-version of the above as well, which will not be needed. We leave the details to the reader. In fact, the whole construction works for unbounded complexes and so
the other argument is dual.

Proof.

Let
\mu\in\underline{\Coh}(F,G)_{0}
be a coherent transformation. We have to define morphisms

\alpha_{n}:s^{-1}F\to(s^{-1}G)^{\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}n}\qquad n\geq 1

of degree 0 satisfying
\dd\alpha=\alpha\dd
(for the extension of
\alpha:=\mlq\prod\mrq\alpha_{n}
to
\mlq T^{\prod}\mrq(s^{-1}X)\to\mlq T^{\prod}\mrq(s^{-1}Y)
), or
more elementarily:

\sum_{i+j=k}(\alpha_{i}\otimes\alpha_{j})\dd_{2}+\alpha_{k+1}\dd_{1}=\dd_{2}\alpha_{k}+\dd_{1}\alpha_{k+1}.

(95)

Consider the set of factorizations of
p_{k+1}:[1]\to[k+1]
in
\Delta^{\circ}_{\act}=\text{\footnotesize$\mathcal{O}$}^{\circ,\op}_{\act}
of the form

\textstyle{[1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{i_{k-1}+1}}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[k]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{i_{0}+1}}
\textstyle{[k+1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[k+1]}

in
\Delta_{\act}^{\circ,\op}
, i.e. a sequence of injections with endpoints fixed, where all injections are non-identities, and thus necessarily of the form
\delta_{i}
.
We have
0\leq i_{n}<k-n
. As in (
5.56
) we see them either as sequences
\underline{i}=(i_{0},\dots,i_{k-1})
or as a leveled tree.
In total, there are
k!
such factorizations which constitute non-degenerate
k
-simplices of
N_{p_{k+1}}([1]\times_{/\Delta^{\circ}_{\act}}\Delta^{\circ}_{\act}\times_{/\Delta^{\circ}_{\act}}[k+1])_{k}
.

Each
\mu(\underline{i})
defines an element in
\underline{\Hom}_{k}^{\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}}(F,G^{\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}k+1})
which we consider as a degree
k
morphism

F\to G^{\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}(k+1)}
.
We can thus define a degree 0 morphism:

\displaystyle\alpha_{k+1}:s^{-1}F

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle(s^{-1}G)^{\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}k+1}

\displaystyle s^{-1}x

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle(s^{-1})^{\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}(k+1)}\sum_{\underline{i}}\sgn(\underline{i})\mu(\underline{i})(x)

where is sum is over all
\underline{i}=(i_{0},\dots,i_{k-1})
with

and where we used the Koszul sign convention (
6.13
) and where

\mathrm{sgn}(\underline{i}):=(-1)^{\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}i_{j}}.

\alpha_{1}
is just the degree 0 map
F\to G
evaluation at
[1]\to[1]\to[1]
.

We a left to show (
95
).
Define for each
m=1,\dots,k-1
an involution
\sigma_{m}
on these factorizations given by

(\delta_{i},\delta_{j})\mapsto\begin{cases}(\delta_{j-1},\delta_{i})&i<j\\
(\delta_{j},\delta_{i+1})&i\geq j\end{cases}

at positions
m,m+1
.
Each involution
\sigma_{m}
changes the sign:

\mathrm{sgn}(\sigma_{m}(\underline{i}))=-\mathrm{sgn}(\underline{i}).

(96)

Since the maps
\mu_{k}:N_{p_{k+1}}([1]\times_{/\Delta^{\circ}_{\act}}\Delta^{\circ}_{\act}\times_{/\Delta^{\circ}_{\act}}[k+1])_{k}\to\underline{\Hom}^{\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}}_{k}(F,G^{\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}(k+1)})
are simplicial, we have
66
66

66

using
\dd(s^{-1})^{\otimes k+1}=(-1)^{k}(s^{-1})^{\otimes k+1}\dd
.
:

\displaystyle(-1)^{k}(\dd\alpha_{k+1}-\alpha_{k+1}\dd)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{k}(s^{-1})^{\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}(k+1)}\sum_{\underline{i}}(-1)^{j}\sgn(\underline{i})\mu(\delta_{j}\underline{i})s

\displaystyle=\sum_{\underline{i}}(s^{-1})^{\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}(k+1)}\sgn(\underline{i})\mu(\delta_{0}\underline{i})s+\sum_{\underline{i}}(s^{-1})^{\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}(k+1)}(-1)^{k}\sgn(\underline{i})\mu(\delta_{k}\underline{i})s

because

for

and the sign property (

).

\delta_{0}\underline{i}
is equal to

\textstyle{[1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{[k+1].}

under the composition

\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{Aw}}
\textstyle{(\underline{\Hom}(F,G^{\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}a+1})\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}\underline{\Hom}(F,G^{\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}b+1}))_{[k]}}

\textstyle{\underline{\Hom}_{[k]}(F,G^{\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}k+1})}

where the last map is the composition with the comultiplication
F\to F^{\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}2}
. By Lemma
5.29
thus
to the morphism

F\to G^{\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}k+1}

of degree
k
, given by

(\mu(i_{1}^{\prime})\mathbin{\tilde{\otimes}}\mu(i_{2}^{\prime}))F(m)

with Koszul sign convention, if

and
